# Project: Enūma eliš, Tablets I–VII → Hebrew

Read `../../translation-guidelines.md` first, then `../corpus.md`, `../renderings.md` (corpus, binding; it inherits the flood corpus's shared renderings), `renderings.md` (this project: the eleven monsters, the fixed repeated passages), then this file.

## Scope
- **Text:** *Enūma eliš* ("When on high"), the Babylonian Epic of Creation, Standard Babylonian, **all seven tablets**, 1,098 lines in the eBL edition (I 162, II 162, III 138, IV 146, V 158, VI 166, VII 166).
- **Story:** the primeval waters Apsû and Tiāmat and the birth of the gods (I 1–28); Apsû's plan to destroy the noisy gods, and Ea's victory over him (I 29–78); the birth of Marduk (I 79–108); Tiāmat roused, her army and Qingu (I 109–162); the gods' fear: Ea and Anu fail (II); Marduk's conditions and the message to Laḫmu and Laḫamu (III); Marduk's enthronement, the battle, the splitting of Tiāmat and the making of the sky (IV); the stars, the calendar, the clouds, rivers and mountains from Tiāmat's body (V); the creation of man from Qingu's blood, the building of Babylon and Esagil, the bow, the first names (VI); the fifty names and the epilogue (VII).
- **Work is split by tablet.** Each tablet has its own working files (`annotated-I.rtl.md` … `annotated-VII.rtl.md`, `translation-I.rtl.md` … `translation-VII.rtl.md`) and its own section below. `translation.rtl.md` (the whole poem) is **generated** by `build_translation.py`.

## Base text
- **eBL corpus, `L/1/2`** (*Poem of Creation*), Standard Babylonian I–VII: edition A. C. Heinrich, revision Z. J. Földi and E. Jiménez; translation B. R. Foster (with A. A. Fadhil). Folders `../../sources/eBL/Enuma-elish-SB-I/` … `-VII/` (gitignored), generated by `tools/ebl_fetch.py` on 2026-10-16. Start with each folder's `reading-edition.md`; the stems are in `glossary.md`; the notes cite the bibliography in `bibliography.md`.
- **Variant reconstructions** (`N (variant, other manuscripts)` in `reading-edition.md`): translate the main reconstruction; give the variant in the apparatus, and translate it there when it changes the sense (e.g. I 40 *[…] mūšiš lū nēḫet* "[…] by night let her / may you be at rest").
- **Comparison (rank 3, after forming our own reading):** the eBL English (Foster), cited as "eBL". **Rank 5 (history only):** King 1902 (`../../sources/King-1902--Seven-Tablets-of-Creation/`), Langdon 1923 (`../../sources/Langdon-1923--Babylonian-Epic-of-Creation/`). Lambert 2013, Kämmerer & Metzler 2012 and Talon 2005 are paid and were not used; Heidel 1951 (*The Babylonian Genesis*) is © and was not used.
- **Hebrew, register only:** none consulted (Shifra & Klein 1996 is ©, not owned).

## Line numbering
eBL (Heinrich) numbering, which is that of Lambert 2013 for most lines. The older literature (King, Langdon, Heidel, ANET) numbers differently in II–III, V and VI–VII; the apparatus gives the old number when a parallel in the literature depends on it. eBL's `oldLineNumbers` field (in `_raw/chapter-display.json`) gives the concordance where eBL has one.

## Parallels with Genesis to watch for
A preliminary list (from the relevance survey and a first reading of the edition), to be checked in the parallels index of each tablet. The relevant commentary file is `../../../../פירוש/1-בראשית/1010-בראשית-א_א-ב_ג-שבע_ימי_הבריאה.rtl.md` (the commentary reads `תְהוֹם`, `רָקִיעַ … מַבְדִּיל`, `וַיִּקְרָא`, the luminaries, `הַתַּנִּינִם הַגְּדֹלִים`, `נֶפֶשׁ חַיָּה`, `בְּצַלְמֵנוּ`, `וַיִּשְׁבֹּת`, `מְלַאכְתּוֹ`, `צְבָאָם`, and the contrast `בָּרָא` / `עָשָׂה`).
- **I 1–8** "When on high the heavens had not been named …": creation as naming ↔ `וַיִּקְרָא` (1:5, 8, 10); the "when … not yet" opening ↔ 2:4b–5.
- **I 3–5** Apsû and Tiāmat mingling their waters ↔ `תְהוֹם` (1:2) [cognate], `מַיִם … מַיִם` (1:6–7).
- **I 15–16** *umaššil* / *tamšīlu*: Anu his father's equal; Nudimmud begotten as Anu's likeness ↔ `וַיּוֹלֶד בִּדְמוּתוֹ כְּצַלְמוֹ` (5:3).
- **I 29–78** the gods' noise disturbs Apsû's rest; Apsû's plan; Ea casts sleep on him, kills him and *rests* in his dwelling ↔ rest (2:2–3); Atra-ḫasīs's noise.
- **I 79–108** Marduk's birth "in the heart of Apsû"; Anu creates the four winds and gives them to Marduk; Marduk "stirs up" (*dalāḫu*) Tiāmat ↔ `וְרוּחַ אֱלֹהִים מְרַחֶפֶת עַל פְּנֵי הַמָּיִם` (1:2) [divergence].
- **I 129–162** (= II 15–48, III 19–52, III 77–110) Tiāmat's eleven monsters, brought into being (*uštabši*) by the chaos mother ↔ `וַיִּבְרָא אֱלֹהִים אֶת הַתַּנִּינִם הַגְּדֹלִים` (1:21). **The unit the commentary is editing now.**
- **IV 19–26** the constellation destroyed and restored by Marduk's word ("command, and let them be firm") ↔ creation by word, `וַיְהִי כֵן`.
- **IV 35–60, 93–104** Marduk's weapons: the bow, the net, the seven winds, the Deluge (*abūbu*) ↔ Ex 14–15; the wind into Tiāmat's mouth.
- **IV 135–146** Tiāmat split "like a fish for drying"; half of her set up as a roof of heaven, the skin stretched, guards posted so that her waters should not go out ↔ the `רָקִיעַ` dividing the waters (1:6–8). Akkadian has no *raqīʿu*: see `../renderings.md` §3.
- **V 1–46** the stations of the great gods, the stars, the year, the twelve months, the moon (*Nannaru*, "luminary") and its phases, the seventh day, *šapattu*, the sun ↔ 1:14–18 (`לְאֹתֹת וּלְמוֹעֲדִים וּלְיָמִים וְשָׁנִים`; the unnamed `מְאוֹרֹת`).
- **V 47–66** clouds, rain, mist from Tiāmat's spittle; the springs; Euphrates and Tigris from her eyes; mountains on her breasts; "he shaped heaven and earth" ↔ 1:9–10; 2:6, 10–14; 1:1.
- **V 73–76** the images (*ṣalmu*) of the eleven monsters at the gate of Apsû, "a sign (*ittu*) never to be forgotten" ↔ `צֶלֶם`, `אוֹת` (1:26; 9:12–17).
- **VI 1–38** man (*lullû*, "let 'man' be his name") made from Qingu's blood, to bear the gods' toil so that they may rest ↔ 1:26–28; 2:7, 15; 5:2.
- **VI 39–46** Marduk divides the Anunnakkū between heaven and earth ↔ Deut 32:8.
- **VI 49–66** the gods build Babylon; bricks made for a year; Esagil's "head raised" ↔ 11:3–4; the shrine as a "resting place" ↔ Ps 132:14.
- **VI 82–91** the bow set in heaven as a star ↔ 9:13.
- **VI 121 – VII** the fifty names; naming as an act of rule ↔ 2:19–20; Ex 34:5–7.

## Decisions (coordinator, before drafting)
1. **Corpus renderings** (`../renderings.md`) and **project renderings** (`renderings.md`) were fixed before drafting: the names (Tiāmat `תִּיאָמַת`, Apsû `אַפְּסוּ`/`הָאַפְּסוּ`, Kakka `כַּכָּא`, Nannaru `נַנָּרוּ` …), the naming verbs (*nabû* `קָרָא`, *šuma zakāru* `הִזְכִּיר שֵׁם`), the sky (*ṣullulu* `קֵרָה`; no `רָקִיעַ`), division (*parāsu* `הִפְרִיד`, *zâzu* `חִלֵּק`), rest (*nâḫu* `נָח`, *pašāḫu* `נִרְגַּע`), *ṣalmu* `צֶלֶם`, *tamšīlu* `תַּבְנִית`, the eleven monsters, and the full Hebrew of Tiāmat's army (I 129–162).
2. **Sequential drafting by tablet** (I → VII), one translator at a time. Each tablet's translator receives the Hebrew of all earlier lines that its tablet repeats.
3. **Section headings:** follow eBL's `isBeginningOfSection` (the `---` lines in `reading-edition.md`) and add descriptive Hebrew headings (`## 1–28: …`) in both files.
4. **After Tablet I (coordinator review):** (a) I 156 = II 42 = III 46 = III 104 is now `יגדלו מאמריך על כולם, על האנונכי!` (apposition, not vocative; the Tablet I translator's proposal 5). (b) Brackets in repeated lines follow each tablet's own manuscripts (guidelines §6.8 and the flood corpus rule: a word is bracketed when its root, or most of it, is broken in all manuscripts of that tablet, even if a parallel line preserves it). So I 144 `[מלחמה]` (eBL *tā[hāz]i*) is bracketed, I 131 *[na]šû* (one sign) is not, and the copies in III carry their own brackets (revised after Tablet III; `verify.py` accepts bracket-only differences in repeated lines and lists them). (c) The Tablet I translator's terms in `renderings.md` §4 (*šasû* = `צָעַק`, *ṣalālu* = `יָשֵׁן`, *sakāpu* = `שָׁכַב`, *karšu* = `בֶּטֶן`, *lemnu* = `אוֹיֵב`, *gērû* = `צַר` …) are binding for II–VII. (d) After Tablet IV: *wabālu* = `הוֹבִיל` corpus-wide, but III 56 = III 114 *libbašu âra ubla* keeps `לבו נשא (אותו) לגשת`, because the Hebrew idiom `נְשָׂאוֹ לִבּוֹ` (שמות לה:כא) renders the Akkadian idiom exactly; a stated exception, not a precedent.

## Open decisions for the user
1. **Kakka / Gaga** (Anšar's vizier, Tablet III): eBL reads *Kakka*; the Hebrew `קַקָּה` reads as a nursery word, so the translation uses the older reading `גַּגָּא` with a note. Alternative: `כַּכָּה` or `קַקָּא`. **Resolved 2026-10-23** (`../../harmonization--2026-10-23.md` row 20): `כַּכָּא` (eBL's *Kakka*, a final aleph so that it reads neither `קקה` nor `ככה`; always vocalized), as in Nergal and Ereškigal.
2. **Marduk** = `מַרְדּוּךְ` (the current Hebrew form), not the biblical `מְרֹדָךְ` (Jer 50:2), which is noted at the first occurrence. Same policy as Ištar/`עַשְׁתֹּרֶת`.
3. **Apsû with and without the article** (`אַפְּסוּ` the god, `הָאַפְּסוּ` the place, from I 71): an interpretive choice that the Akkadian does not mark.
4. **Corpus-wide: *šipru* "work".** Enūma eliš renders it `פֹּעַל` (Tablets V–VII), Atra-ḫasīs I renders it `מְלָאכָה`. `מְלָאכָה` pre-empts `מְלַאכְתּוֹ` (בראשית ב:ב–ג); `פֹּעַל` hides the semantic link "work" ← "send" (*šapāru* / לאך), which the apparatus states (VI 37). Decide one word for both corpora. **Settled by the user, 2026-10-23: `מְלָאכָה` everywhere** (V 63, 141, VI 37, 57, 67 changed, tagged [equivalent] Gen 2:2–3).
5. **Cognate density in the text (Tablet VII decision 4):** VII renders several cognates in the text itself (`הַמּוֹצִיא יֶרֶק`, `מַדְשִׁיא`, `אֵד`, `רְכוּבוֹ`), as the echo rule allows when the meaning matches; the alternative is neutral words with the cognates only in the notes.
6. **The fifty names:** Asarluḫi as `אֲסַרְלוּחִי` (eBL) or the familiar `אֲסַלּוּחִי`; the hyphenation policy (Tablet VII decision); Nēberu counted as the 50th name (Foster's count is one lower from 36 on).
7. **VII 74, 128:** eBL lemmatizes the common noun "sea" (`הַיָּם`), Foster reads the goddess Tiāmat.
8. **Per-tablet open decisions:** each tablet section below has its own list (I: 8, II: 10, III: 8, IV: 10, V: 12, VI: 11, VII: 11). Most are single cruxes or a choice between two Hebrew words; none blocks the use of the translation.

## Working rules for the tablet translators
- Follow guidelines §5 step 5 for **every line**: read N, L, notes and variants in `reading-edition.md`; parse every word (lemma, stem, tense, ventive, subjunctive, *-ma*; stems from `glossary.md`); form your own rendering; **only then** read the eBL translation (T) and record disagreements; run the echo check (§6.4) with the tags.
- Format exactly as `../../סיפורי-המבול-המסופוטמיים/1-אתרחסיס/annotated-I.rtl.md` and guidelines §7.2: `### <line>`, the normalization in backticks, `> ` Hebrew line, then `- **פירוק:**`, `- **תרגומים:**`, `- **הקבלה:**` (when relevant), `- **הערות:**` (when relevant). The apparatus is in Hebrew.
- Draft unvocalized, plene; vocalize names at first occurrence and ambiguous words (guidelines §5, §6.2).
- Repeated lines: copy the Hebrew of the first occurrence exactly (`renderings.md` §2–3) and mark `[מקבילה: I 129]`.
- `translation-<T>.rtl.md`: a short Hebrew introduction to the tablet, `##` section headings, numbered lines `**<n>** <Hebrew>` followed by two spaces (line break), footnotes only for major cruxes and key parallels. Its numbered lines must equal the annotated `>` lines (`verify.py` checks this).
- Run `python3 -I ../../tools/check_bible_quotes.py <files>` and fix every mismatch. **The repository's Bible has its own verse division in places** (e.g. Exodus 20: `וַיָּנַח בַּיּוֹם הַשְּׁבִיעִי` is שמות כ:י); always quote with the vocalization of `תנך-מנוקד/`.
- Record in this file, under the tablet's section: scope, sources consulted, decisions, progress log (incl. the three review passes), the tablet's **parallels index** (table: Line | Akkadian | Hebrew rendering | Bible | Tag | Comment), and "Proposals for the shared renderings".

## Tablet I

### Scope
Tablet I, eBL lines 1–162 (eBL has no section breaks for this tablet; the Hebrew headings are ours):
- **1–20** "When on high": the unnamed heavens and earth, Apsû and Mummu-Tiāmat mingling their waters, no reed-meadow yet; the gods "built" (*ibbanû*) inside them, generation by generation: Laḫmu and Laḫamu, Anšar and Kišar, Anu, Nudimmud (= Ea), each greater than the last.
- **21–28** the young gods' noise and dancing disturb Tiāmat; Apsû cannot reduce it; Tiāmat is silent.
- **29–58** Apsû and his vizier Mummu take counsel before Tiāmat: "by day I have no rest, by night I do not sleep"; Apsû wants to destroy the gods; Tiāmat refuses ("shall we destroy what we built?"); Mummu urges him on; the gods hear and fall silent.
- **59–78** Ea's spell: he pours sleep on Apsû, strips him of sash, crown and radiance, kills him, binds Mummu, founds his dwelling on Apsû, rests in his chamber, names the place "Apsû", and dwells there with Damkina.
- **79–104** Marduk is born ("built") in the Apsû; his superhuman body (four eyes, four ears, fire from his lips); "Mār-Utu … the son, the sun of the gods"; ten gods' radiance, fifty terrors.
- **105–110** Anu creates the four winds and gives them to Marduk to play with; Marduk makes dust and a storm, brings a wave into being and roils Tiāmat; the gods inside her get no rest.
- **111–128** the gods complain to Tiāmat (Apsû's death, the winds, their sleeplessness) and urge her to war; she agrees.
- **129–162** the fixed passage (Tiāmat's army, the eleven creatures, Qingu's elevation and the tablet of destinies), copied verbatim from `renderings.md` §2, with the full apparatus.

Files: `annotated-I.rtl.md` (162 entries), `translation-I.rtl.md` (introduction, 8 section headings, 162 lines, 18 footnotes).

### Sources consulted
- eBL `L/1/2` SB I: `reading-edition.md` (N, variants, notes, parallels, ATF of ~75 manuscripts), `glossary.md` (stems), `manuscripts.md`, `_raw/chapter-display.json` (checked: no `isBeginningOfSection`, no `oldLineNumbers` for Tablet I).
- eBL English (Foster/Fadhil) as rank-3 comparison, after drafting.
- King 1902 vol. 1 (history only: lines 1–21, the "chaos" reading of *mummu*, "no field was formed", "in the midst [of heaven]").
- The flood corpus: Atra-ḫasīs I (noise and sleeplessness, 352–359), shared renderings.
- The repository's vocalized Bible (`תנך-מנוקד/`) via `tools/check_bible_quotes.py --show`.
- Not used: Lambert 2013, Talon, Kämmerer & Metzler (paid); Langdon 1923 (not needed for Tablet I).

### Decisions
1. **Naming verbs**: *nabû* = `קָרָא (בְּשֵׁם)` (I 1, 76); *šuma zakāru* = `הִזְכִּיר שֵׁם` / passive `הֻזְכַּר בְּשֵׁם` (I 2, 8, 10). To keep `קָרָא` exclusive for *nabû* (the verb behind `וַיִּקְרָא` of Gen 1), ***šasû* "to shout, call (to)" = `צָעַק`** (I 30, 42, 43).
2. **I 1–2**: *eliš/šapliš* = `ממעל/ממטה`; *ammatu* = `קַרְקַע`; *šamāmū* = `שמים` (noted as poetic).
3. **I 3–4, 71**: Apsû without the article while he is a person (3–70, 113, 117), `הָאַפְּסוּ` from I 71 (the place; 76 names it). `מוּמּוּ־תִּיאָמַת` in I 4 with the note on *mummu*.
4. **I 6 / I 77 *gipāru***: the same eBL lemma (gipāru I) has two senses: I 6 "reed-meadow" = `אָחוּ` (with *ṣuṣû* = `סְבַךְ־קָנִים`), I 77 "living quarters (the giparu)" = `מְעוֹן`. The possible wordplay is noted in I 77.
5. **I 9, 12, 81–83, 89, 128**: *banû* = `בָּנָה` throughout, also of birth (`נִבְנוּ`, `נִבְנָה מַרְדּוּךְ`, `בְּנָה אותו אאה`, *bānû* = `בּוֹנֶה`); never `בָּרָא`.
6. **I 15**: taken with **Anšar as subject** ("Anšar made Anu, his offspring, like (himself)"), parallel to I 16; eBL has Anu as subject. Both in the note.
7. **I 16** *tamšīlu* = `תַּבְנִית`; *ulid* = `הוליד`; the I 16 ↔ Gen 5:3 parallel is explicit in the apparatus and footnote 5.
8. **Rest/sleep vocabulary** (the background of Gen 2:2–3): *pašāḫu* G `נִרְגַּע`, Š `הִרְגִּיעַ`, Š stative `רָגוּעַ` (38, 50, 63, 110), adverb *šupšuḫiš* = `בְּמַרְגּוֹעַ` (75); *nâḫu* G `נָח` (75; variants of 40 and 50), D `הֵנִיחַ` (161); ***ṣalālu* = `יָשֵׁן` only** (G, Š = Piel `יִשֵּׁן`), because the fixed passage gives *sakāpu* = `שָׁכַב` (130; so also 120, 122); *šittu* = `שֵׁנָה`; *qūlu* = `דְּמָמָה`. `נחת` is avoided (root נוח): *ṭūbātiš* = `בְּשַׁלְוָה` (64).
9. **I 23, 108–109, 116, 119** *dalāḫu*: G `דָּלַח` (23, 108), stative `דְּלוּחָה` (109), Š `הִדְלִיחַ` (116), adverb *dulluḫiš* `בְּדִלּוּחַ` (119).
10. ***karšu* = `בֶּטֶן`** (23, 44, 111, 116), with the cognate `כָּרֵשׂ` (Jer 51:34) in the note: `כרס` sounds comic in modern Hebrew, and biblical `בֶּטֶן` also carries thoughts (Job 15:35).
11. **I 27** *elīšun* = "on them" (Apsû and Tiāmat), as all manuscripts read; eBL translates "to her".
12. **I 64** *reḫû* (sleep) = `נָסַךְ` (Isa 29:10); I 80 *ittarḫi* (conception) = `הֹרָה` (Job 3:3).
13. **I 76** *imbīšum-ma apsû* = `קרא לו "אפסו"` – the Gen 1:5 construction.
14. **I 81** Marduk = `מַרְדּוּךְ`, first-occurrence note on `מְרֹדָךְ` (Jer 50:2) and `בֵּל` (Isa 46:1). I 80 *bēlu* = `הָאָדוֹן` (common noun).
15. **I 91** *ilūtu* = `אֵלוּת` (not `אֱלֹהוּת`, to keep *ilu* = `אֵל`).
16. **I 105, 115** *šār erbetti* = `אַרְבַּע הָרוּחוֹת`; both lines begin `בנה את ארבע הרוחות`.
17. **I 124** *zāqīqu* = `הֶבֶל` (`רוּחַ` stays for *šāru*). **I 106** *mēlulu* = `שִׂחֵק`. **I 107** *eperu* = `עָפָר` [cognate].
18. **I 129–162**: the fixed Hebrew of `renderings.md` §2, unchanged; apparatus as listed under that table, plus notes on 133 (`עִצֵּב` ≠ `עֶצֶב`), 136/141/143 (Deut 32:33), 138 (`כֵּאלֹהִים`, Gen 3:5), 146.
19. eBL's lemma glosses were overridden where the form demanded it: I 10 vs 112 *izzakrū* (N "were named" vs Gt "spoke"); I 67 *agû* "crown" vs I 108 *agû* "wave"; I 13 *ūmū* "days" vs I 143 *ūmū* "storms"; I 110 *šārīša* (eBL lemma *šāriš* "in the wind" vs eBL translation "her winds").

### Open decisions for the user
1. **I 6 / I 77 *gipāru***: `אָחוּ` (meadow) and `מְעוֹן` (Ea's residence) – two Hebrew words for one Akkadian word. Alternative: a transliteration `גִּפָּר` in I 77, to keep the link visible in the text.
2. **I 15**: Anšar as subject (our choice) or Anu as subject (eBL).
3. **I 48** is a crux (`וזיר שאינו מסכים – *עצת מומו שלו*`): either Mummu's advice was disloyal (eBL), or "the vizier not agreeing with the counsel of his *mummu*" (= Tiāmat, cf. I 4).
4. **Brackets in the fixed passage**: in Tablet I, eBL restores part of the root in I 131 *[na]šû* and I 144 *tā[hāz]i*, but the fixed Hebrew (`נושאים`, `מלחמה`) has no brackets, and `verify.py` requires the copy to be identical. Options: (a) leave as is (the words are certain from the copies in II–III); (b) allow `[נו]שאים` / `מלח[מה]` in Tablet I only and teach `verify.py` that exception.
5. **I 156** fixed Hebrew `יגדלו מאמריך על כולם, האנונכי!` reads as a vocative; the Akkadian *anukkī* is in apposition to *kalîšunu* ("over all of them, the Anunnakkū"). Proposal: `יגדלו מאמריך על כולם, על האנונכי!` (would change II 42, III 46, III 104 too).
6. **I 40 variant** *[…] mūšiš lū nēḫet*: the form is a stative 3fs (or short 2ms), not "may I rest" (project.md, base text section). Translated in the note as "[…] בלילה *יהי נח*".
7. ***karšu* = `בֶּטֶן`** rather than the cognate `כָּרֵס` (decision 10).
8. **I 110** *šārīša*: "in the wind" (eBL lemma, our text) or "her winds" (eBL translation).

| 157 | *ṭuppi šīmāti* | לוח הגורלות | `וְהָיוּ לְאֹתֹת וּלְמוֹעֲדִים` (א:יד) | [absent] / [divergence] | |
| 158 | *likūn ṣīt pîka* | ייכון מוצא פיך | `כִּי עַל כָּל מוֹצָא פִי יְהוָה יִחְיֶה הָאָדָם` (דברים ח:ג); `וַיְהִי כֵן` (א:ז) | [cognate] (*aṣû* = יצא; *kânu* = כון) / [divergence] | The power of the word is conferred on Qingu (and later taken away), not inherent. |
| 161 | *gīra linīḫḫa* | יניח את האש | `וַהֲנִחוֹתִי חֲמָתִי בָּם` (יחזקאל ה:יג) | [cognate] (*nâḫu* D) | |

### Lines shared with other tablets
- I 31 → III 3 (with Kakka for Mummu; the Akkadian differs in the name).
- I 73 → IV 123 (exact, per eBL).
- I 74 → cf. IV 125.
- I 102 → cf. VI 127.
- I 127 → cf. II 13, III 17, III 75.
- I 129–162 → II 15–48, III 19–52, III 77–110 (the fixed passage).
- Internal to I: 27 ~ 37; 38 ~ 50 (~ 110); 81 ~ 82; 105 ~ 115; 23 ~ 116; 113 ~ 117; I 161 cf. I 96 (*gīru*). eBL also gives external parallels outside Enūma eliš: I 11 cf. L I.9 SB II 35–36; I 22–23 cf. L I.10 SB I a+39.

**Hebrew of lines that later tablets repeat** (final):
- I 31: `מומו, הוזיר, המיטיב את כְּבֵדִי,`
- I 73: `אחרי אשר את אויביו אסר, הכה,`
- I 74: `אאה העמיד את נצחונו על צריו,`
- I 102: `הבן – שֶׁמֶשׁ, שמש הא[לים]!`
- I 127: `סבבו אותה האלים בקרבה,`

### Proposals for the shared renderings
For the corpus `renderings.md` (or the project §4, which has received them):
- ***šasû* = `צָעַק`**, so that `קָרָא` stays exclusive for *nabû* (Gen 1 `וַיִּקְרָא`).
- ***ṣalālu* = `יָשֵׁן` only** (Š = Piel `יִשֵּׁן`); ***sakāpu* = `שָׁכַב`** (the fixed passage already has it). The corpus row "*ṣalālu* = `שָׁכַב (לִישֹׁן)` / `יָשֵׁן`" should be narrowed, otherwise I 120–122 and 130 collide with the sleep verb.
- ***karšu* = `בֶּטֶן`** (cognate `כָּרֵשׂ` in a note).
- *naṣīru* = `צְוָחָה` (beside *rigmu* = `שָׁאוֹן`, *ḫubūru* = `הֲמֻלָּה`); *qūlu* = `דְּמָמָה`; *šittu* = `שֵׁנָה`.
- *dâlu* = `נָע`; *alaktu* = `דֶּרֶךְ`; *kapādu* = `זָמַם`; *kamû* = `אָסַר` (N `נֶאֱסַר`); *nêru* = `הָרַג`; *lemnu* (foe) = `אוֹיֵב`; *gērû* = `צַר`; *ernittu* = `נִצָּחוֹן`.
- *ḫarmu* = `רֵעַ` (consort); *ḫāʾiru* = `בַּעַל`; *ḫīrtu* = `רַעְיָה`.
- *atāru* = `יָתַר`, Š stative *šūtur* = `יַתִּיר`; *nakālu* D = `חִכֵּם`, stative `מְחֻכָּם` (cognate נכל noted).
- *kummu* = `חֶדֶר`; *kiṣṣu* = `קֹדֶשׁ`; *atmanu* = `דְּבִיר`; *ešertu* = `מִקְדָּשׁ`; *gipāru* (building) = `מְעוֹן`.
- *ilūtu* = `אֵלוּת`; *ištartu* (common noun) = `אֵלָה`.
- *mēlulu* = `שִׂחֵק`; *zāqīqu* = `הֶבֶל`; *abšānu* = `מוֹטָה` (*ullu* stays `עֹל`); *eperu* = `עָפָר`; *agû* II (wave) = `גַּל`.
- *reḫû* (sleep) = `נָסַךְ`; *tû* = `לַחַשׁ`; *ṣerretu* = `חֶבֶל הָאַף`; *sukkallu* = `וְזִיר`; *tamlāku* = `יוֹעֵץ`; *milku* = `עֵצָה`; *šāninu* = `מִתְחָרֶה`; *bānû* = `בּוֹנֶה`.
- Names: Andurunna = `אַנְדּוּרוּנָּה`; Māri-Utu = `מָרִי־אוּתוּ`.
- Fixed passage: see open decisions 4–5 (I 131/144 brackets; I 156 punctuation).
- `check_bible_quotes.py` on `renderings.md` reports 3 mismatches in §1–§2 (coordinator's rows, not changed here): lemma forms in backticks directly before a reference (§1 row 3 `פֶּתֶן` before דברים לב:לג; §2 notes `הִמְשִׁיל` before ישעיהו מו:ה and `מִלֵּא אֶת יָדוֹ` before שמות כח:מא). Suggest writing them unvocalized or quoting the verse (`פְּתָנִים`, `וְתַמְשִׁלוּנִי`, `וּמִלֵּאתָ אֶת יָדָם`). The rows added to §4 by Tablet I pass.

### Lessons for the guidelines
- **eBL lemmas can join homonyms or senses that the Hebrew must separate** (I 6 / 77 *gipāru* "meadow" / "giparu"), and one surface form can belong to two stems (I 10 *izzakrū* N "were named" / I 112 Gt "spoke"). Parse the form and the context, then decide whether one Hebrew word or two.
- **Check the edition's translation against the transliterations themselves**: in I 27 all manuscripts read *e-li-šu-un* "on them", while eBL translates "to her".
- **Reserve the Hebrew word of a key Genesis comparison for its Akkadian counterpart only**, and move other verbs away from it even when they are natural (here `קָרָא` for *nabû* only; *šasû* = `צָעַק`). Likewise avoid Hebrew words that share the reserved root (`נחת` from נוח).
- **A fixed passage drafted before a tablet should be checked against that tablet's brackets** (here I 131, 144), since the first copy may be the most damaged.
- In `translation-*.rtl.md`, do not start an introduction line with `**…**`: `verify.py` reads it as a numbered verse line.

## Tablet II

### Scope
Tablet II, eBL lines 1–162 (eBL has no section breaks and no old line numbers for this tablet; the Hebrew headings are ours):
- **1–4** Tiāmat gathers her creatures (*pitqu*, "those she shaped") and draws up battle against the gods, her offspring; she is now worse than Apsû; Ea is told.
- **5–14** Ea is stunned, ponders, calms down and goes to Anšar; he repeats Tiāmat's plot: "our mother hates us … even those you built go at her side" (11–14 = III 15–18 = III 73–76).
- **15–48** the fixed passage (= I 129–162): Tiāmat's army, the eleven creatures, Qingu and the tablet of destinies, copied verbatim from `renderings.md` §2, with a short apparatus pointing to Tablet I.
- **49–70** Anšar's alarm and reproach ("you started the battle … bear it yourself"); Ea's soothing answer: Anšar "who has the power to bring into being and to destroy"; had Ea not killed Apsû, who would be alive to see this?
- **71–94** Anšar sends Ea to calm Tiāmat with his spell; Ea sees her and turns back; his report (her strength, her assembly, her war-cry; "a woman's strength is not that of a male"; "send another").
- **95–118** Anšar sends Anu to calm her with entreaty; Anu fails; his report repeats Ea's word for word (103–118 = 79–94).
- **119–128** Anšar stares at the ground; the Igigi and Anunnaki sit with covered lips; no god will go against Tiāmat.
- **129–154** Ea summons Marduk to his secret place and sends him to Anšar; Marduk: "what male has brought battle against you? It is Tiāmat, a woman … you will soon tread on her neck"; Anšar: "calm Tiāmat with your pure spell, drive the chariot of the storms".
- **155–162** Marduk's conditions (156–162 = III 58–64 = III 116–122): if he is to be the gods' avenger, they must convene the assembly and proclaim his destiny supreme, so that he decrees destinies "by the work of my mouth, in your place", and nothing he builds is changed.

Files: `annotated-II.rtl.md` (162 entries), `translation-II.rtl.md` (introduction, 9 section headings, 162 lines, 16 footnotes).

### Sources consulted
- eBL `L/1/2` SB II: `reading-edition.md` (N, variants, notes, parallels), `glossary.md` (stems), `manuscripts.md` (37 manuscripts), `_raw/chapter-display.json` (checked: no `isBeginningOfSection`, no `oldLineNumbers`). eBL SB III `reading-edition.md` for the Akkadian of III 15–18, 57–64, 73–76, 113–122; SB IV 129 (the treading on Tiāmat).
- eBL English (Foster) as rank-3 comparison, read after drafting.
- King 1902 vol. 1 (history only: Marduk's conditions, old numbering II 127–140 = eBL 149–162, his reading of 151–152).
- The flood corpus renderings and the Tablet I files of this project.
- The repository's vocalized Bible via `tools/check_bible_quotes.py --show`.
- Not used: Lambert 2013, Talon, Kämmerer & Metzler (paid); Langdon 1923 (not needed).

### Decisions
1. **Fixed passage II 15–48** copied character for character from `renderings.md` §2 (by script, normalization from eBL). eBL's partial restorations in II 37–40, 42 (*[šū]t, [ip]qid, [a]ddi, [ma]likūt, [li]rtabbû*) are not bracketed, following the coordinator's decision 4b (the words are certain from I and III). Morphological differences from I (II 27 *(u)*, 34 *ušrabbīš* for *ušrabbīš(u)*, 36 *ananti* for *ananta*) change nothing in the sense; noted in the apparatus.
2. **Rest vocabulary** (the background of Gen 2:2–3): *nâḫu* G `נָח` (7 *uzzašu inūḫu* `וזעמו נח`; 51 `לא נחה בטנו`; 65 `נוּחָה`; 134 `ינוח`); *pašāḫu* G `נִרְגַּע` (72), N `נִרְגַּע` (102 `תֵּרָגַע`), Š `הִרְגִּיעַ` (77, 100, 150); *tapšuḫtu* `מַרְגּוֹעַ`, *tanēḫu* `מְנוּחָה` (59); ***napāšu*** G = `יִנָּפֵשׁ` (100; cognate of `וַיִּנָּפַשׁ`, Ex 31:17), D = `רִוַּח` (132).
3. ***šubšû* / *ḫulluqu*** (62, 64): `להביא לכלל הוויה ולאבד – יש עמו`, with `(הכוח)` added. *ḫulluqu* (D) = `אִבֵּד`, as *ḫalāqu* D in Tablet I (§4), not `הִשְׁמִיד`; Š *ušḫalliqa* (70) = `הֶאֱבִיד`.
4. ***meḫṣu* = `מַחַץ`** (74, 76), not `מַכָּה`: the cognate of `מחץ`, which links the line to Deut 32:39 and Job 26:12.
5. ***šiptu* = `קֶסֶם`** (78, 86, 110), so that `לַחַשׁ` stays for *tû* (39, 150).
6. ***âru* = `נִגַּשׁ (אֶל)`** (55, 88, 112, 123, 144); ***ṭeḫû* = `קָרַב`** (133, 136); ***maḫāriš* = `נֶגֶד`** (124; not `לִקְרַאת`, to keep the letters `קרא` away from *nabû*).
7. ***šuḫarruru* = `הִשְׁתּוֹמֵם`** (6, 82, 106, 119), cf. Ezra 9:3 `וָאֵשְׁבָה מְשׁוֹמֵם`; `דּוֹמֵם`/`דּוּמִיָּה` stay for *šuqammumu*/*šaqummiš*.
8. **The *agāgu* family = `קצף`**: *aggiš* `בְּקֶצֶף` (12), *tušāgigu* `הִקְצַפְתָּ` (56), *uggatu* `קֶצֶף` (78). *uzzu*/*ezēzu* stay `זַעַם`/`זָעַם`; *kamālu* = `כָּעַס` (126); *labābu* = `זָעַף`.
9. **9 = 107 *maḫru abi ālidīšu anšar*** = `נכנס לפני האב, מולידו, אנשר,` for both (eBL translates 9 "grandfather" and 107 "his father who begot him"); 83 *baʾūli* `הנכבד` differs in that word only.
10. **Internal repeats** rendered identically wherever the Akkadian is identical; the 12 differences reported by `verify.py` are all justified: a different word (61/63, 73/75, 81/105, 139/141, 145/147 *abī* / *anšar* / *ea* / *ānu*) or brackets only (74/76, 79/103, 80/104, 82/106, 84/108, 85/109, 87/111, 88/112, 89/113). **94/118** (*ana* / *ina muḫḫīni*) have identical Hebrew: the prepositions are synonyms here.
11. **Brackets** follow eBL's N: a word whose root is restored is bracketed whole (51 `[זועם]`, 84 `[בתחנונים]`, 109 `[במעשיה]`), a partly restored word partly (73 `הולמ[ים]`, 77 `הר[גע]`, 79 `מאמר[י אביו], א[נשר]`, 122 `יש[בו]`, 124 `שפתי[ה]ם`); a restored vowel or a single sign of a certain word is not bracketed (80 *ḫarrānš[ū]*, 89 *šebâm-m[a]*).
12. **Names** vocalized at their first occurrence in II: תִּיאָמַת 1, אַפְּסוּ 3 (the dead god, no article), אֵאָה 4, אַנְשַׁר 8, נוּדִימּוּד 58, אָנוּ 96, הָאִיגִיגִי / הָאֲנוּנָּכִּי 121, מַרְדּוּךְ 128, בְּאוּבְּשׁוּכִּינַּכּוּ 159; קִינְגּוּ and חֻבּוּר are vocalized in the fixed passage. Anu (45) and the Anunnaki (42) first occur inside the fixed passage, which stays unvocalized; they are vocalized at their next occurrence.
13. **Marduk's conditions (156–162)**, the first occurrence of a passage that III repeats twice: *mutīr gimilli* `הַמֵּשִׁיב גְּמוּל` (corpus §2); *akammi* `אֶאֱסֹר`; *uballaṭ* `אֲחַיֶּה`; *šūterā ibâ šīmtī* `רוֹמְמוּ, קִרְאוּ את גורלי` (*nabû* = `קָרָא`; *atāru* Š = `רוֹמֵם`, because `הִגְדִּיל` is *rabû* Š and `הִגְבִּיהַּ` is *šaqû* Š in the fixed passage); *mitḫāriš* `יחדיו`; *epšu pîya kīma kâtunū-ma* `במעשה פי, במקומכם`; *nakāru* Dt `הוּמַר` (161), kept apart from *enû* N `שֻׁנָּה` (162, as 44); *siqar šaptīya* `מאמר שפתיי`.
14. **Other new terms**: *kapātu* D `לִקֵּט` (1); *pitqu* `מְעֻצָּבִים` (1, from *patāqu* `עִצֵּב`); *nipru* `נֵצֶר` (2); *tāḫāza kaṣāru* `קָשַׁר מִלְחָמָה` (2); *ṣamādu* `רָתַם` (4); *šanû* D `תִּנָּה` (10, Aramaic cognate); *ālittu* + suffix `יוֹלַדְתֵּנוּ` (11); *ḫarrānu* `נָתִיב`, *urḫu* `אֹרַח` [cognate], *redû* Št `שָׂם פְּעָמָיו` (8, 80, 104) / `הִנְהִיג` (151); *šibqū* `תַּחְבּוּלוֹת`, *šeʾû* `חָקַר` (81, 86); *unnīnu* `תַּחֲנוּנִים`; *mālaku* `מַהֲלָךְ`; *adīru* `מוֹרָא`; *tukku* `תְּרוּעָה`; *emūqu* `כּוֹחוֹת`; *tēbû* `קִימָה`; *inimmû* `מִלָּה`; *nēmequ* `חָכְמָה`; *tašīmtu* `בִּינָה`; *rubû* `שַׂר`; *qaqqaru* `אַרְצָה` (adverbial, 119); *kašūšu* `*מַפֵּץ*` (97); *kannû* `יַקִּיר`; *gašru* `כַּבִּיר`; *qardu* `אַמִּיץ` (*qarrādu* `גִּבּוֹר`); *elēṣu* `עָלַץ` [cognate]; *alālu* Š `הִתְהַלֵּל` [cognate]; *našāqu* `נָשַׁק`; *kabāsu* `דָּרַךְ` (false friend of `כבש`); *rikbu/rikab* `רֶכֶב` [cognate]; *akāšu* Dt `נִדְחָה`; *maṣû* Š `קִיֵּם`; *meḫṣu* `מַחַץ`; *zikaru* `זָכָר`, *sinništu* `אִשָּׁה`.
15. **eBL lemma slips overridden**: 91 *šupurši* lemmatized as *ṣapru* "a spice" (the identical line 115 has *šapāru* "to send"); 57 *āšiš* lemmatized as *asisû* "copper" (rendered `אוֹגֵר(?)`, "gatherer", with a note). eBL's English swaps 59 and 60; the Hebrew follows the normalization's order.

### Open decisions for the user
1. **9 = 107 *abi ālidīšu***: `האב, מולידו` for both. Alternative: `אבי מולידו` (= "his grandfather") in 9 only, which is literal and true for Ea, but would break the identity with 107.
2. **57 *āšiš milki***: eBL's lemma (*asisû* "copper") does not fit; rendered `אוגר(?) העצה` ("gatherer of counsel"). Foster: "the sage counsellor".
3. **151 *rikab ūmī***: a noun "chariot of the storms" (`את רכב הסערות חיש הנהג`, following Foster) or an imperative "ride the storms" (eBL's lemma is the verb *rakābu*). Both keep the cognate רכב.
4. **155 *bēlū ilī***: plural `אֲדוֹנֵי האלים` (the normalization has *bēlū*, and Marduk addresses the gods in the plural from 156), against Foster's and King's singular ("Lord of the gods", Anšar).
5. **160 *kīma kâtunū-ma***: `במקומכם` ("in your place", Foster, King) or literal `כמוכם` ("like you").
6. ***šiptu* = `קֶסֶם`**: biblical `קֶסֶם` is divination; the alternative `חֶבֶר` (Ps 58:6 `חוֹבֵר חֲבָרִים`) is exact but reads as "friend" unvocalized. **Settled 2026-10-21** (the lead's cross-corpus harmonization): *šiptu* stays `קֶסֶם` (not `חֶבֶר`), with Sum. *nam-šub* / *tu₆* = `קֶסֶם` and Akk. *tû* = `לַחַשׁ` in all corpora.
7. **119 *qaqqara inaṭṭal***: `ארצה מביט` (adverbial ארץ), to avoid `קרקע` (= *ammatu*, I 2) and `אדמה` (Gen 2).
8. **62 *ḫulluqu***: `לאבד` (consistent with *ḫalāqu* D in Tablet I) rather than `להשמיד`.
9. **1 *ukappit … pitiqša***: `לקטה … את מעוצביה`. Alternatives: `קיבצה`/`כינסה` (but `כנס`/`קבץ` are taken for *paḫāru*), `יצוריה` (rejected: pre-empts `וייצר`).
10. **verify.py and brackets**: internal repeats that differ only in brackets are reported as differences (9 of the 12). Proposal: let `verify.py` list bracket-only differences separately, as it does for the fixed passage.

### Lines shared with other tablets
- II 11–14 → III 15–18, III 73–76 (III has *umma* "saying" for *abī* in III 15/73; III 16 *[puḫra šit]kunat-ma* restored).
- II 13 ~ I 127 (cf.; *gimiršun* for *qerebša*).
- II 15–48 = I 129–162 = III 19–52 = III 77–110 (the fixed passage).
- II 156–162 → III 58–64, III 116–122. (II 155 is **not** repeated: III 57 and 115 are different lines, *epšu pîšu ītamâ ana yâti*.)
- Internal: 9 = 107 (cf. 83); 61 ~ 63, 62 = 64; 73 ~ 75, 74 = 76; 79–94 = 103–118 (81/105 differ in the name; 94/118 in the preposition); 135 = 153; 139 ~ 141, 140 = 142; 145 ~ 147, 146 = 148. Cf. also 71 ~ I 125.

**Hebrew of lines that Tablet III repeats** (final; III copies them, changing only what its Akkadian changes):
- II 11: `אבי, תיאמת, יוֹלַדְתֵּנוּ, שונאת אותנו!` (III 15/73 *umma* for *abī*: replace `אבי,` by the III translator's rendering of *umma*.)
- II 12: `אספה הקימה, ובקצף זועפת.`
- II 13: `סבבו אותה האלים כולם,`
- II 14: `אף אלה אשר אתם בניתם – לצדה הלכו.`
- II 156: `אם אני – המשיב גמולכם,`
- II 157: `אֶאֱסֹר את תיאמת ואֲחַיֶּה אתכם,`
- II 158: `הקימו אספה, רוֹמְמוּ, קִרְאוּ את גורלי!`
- II 159: `בְּאוּבְּשׁוּכִּינַּכּוּ, יחדיו, בשמחה תשבו,`
- II 160: `במעשה פי, במקומכם, אגזור גורל:`
- II 161: `לא יוּמַר כל אשר אבנה אני,`
- II 162: `אל ישוב, אל ישונה מאמר שפתיי!`
- (internal, for reference) II 9 = 107: `נכנס לפני האב, מולידו, אנשר,`; II 155 (not repeated): `אֲדוֹנֵי האלים, (אֲדוֹנֵי) גורלות האלים הגדולים:`.
- Note for III: in III 116–122 eBL restores most of the lines; the Hebrew stays identical (brackets per III's own N), as for the fixed passage.

### Proposals for the shared renderings
For the corpus `renderings.md` (§3) or the project §4 (rows added there):
- ***napāšu*** G = `נִנְפַּשׁ` (`יִנָּפֵשׁ`), D = `רִוַּח` [cognate נפש; ↔ `וַיִּנָּפַשׁ`, Ex 31:17] – add to the rest-vocabulary row of corpus §3.
- ***tapšuḫtu* / *tanēḫu*** as a pair ↔ Isa 28:12 `מְנוּחָה … מַרְגֵּעָה` – a note to the *pašāḫu* / *nâḫu* rows.
- ***meḫṣu* / *maḫāṣu* = `מַחַץ` / `מָחַץ`** [cognate] (Deut 32:39; Job 26:12) – for IV (the battle) and later tablets.
- ***kabāsu* = `דָּרַךְ`**, never `כָּבַשׁ` (false friend; already in guidelines §6.7) – IV 129 must follow.
- ***redû*** (all stems of movement/driving) – mark [cognate?] רדה in the apparatus wherever it occurs (Gen 1:26, 28); do not render it רדה.
- ***rakābu* / *rikbu*** = `רָכַב` / `רֶכֶב` [cognate] – for IV 50ff (Marduk's chariot).
- ***šiptu* = `קֶסֶם`; *tû* = `לַחַשׁ`** (open decision 6).
- ***âru* = `נִגַּשׁ`; *ṭeḫû* = `קָרַב`; *šuḫarruru* = `הִשְׁתּוֹמֵם`; *agāgu* family = `קצף`; *kamālu* = `כָּעַס`** – these recur in III–IV.
- ***zikaru* = `זָכָר`; *sinništu* = `אִשָּׁה`** (corpus-wide, for Atra-ḫasīs and VI as well).
- ***nakāru* Dt = `הוּמַר`** vs ***enû* N = `שֻׁנָּה`**.
- ***atāru* Š imperative / active = `רוֹמֵם`** (stative stays `יַתִּיר`).
- Names: none new (Igigi, Anunnaki, Ubšukkinnaku per corpus).

### Lessons for the guidelines
- **Compare the lemmas of identical repeated lines.** eBL lemmatized *šupurši* as *ṣapru* "a spice" in II 91 but correctly as *šapāru* "to send" in the identical II 115; a script that lists differing lemmas for identical normalizations would catch such slips cheaply.
- **The edition's translation can render identical Akkadian differently** (II 9 "grandfather" vs II 107 "his father who begot him"; II 135 "father" vs 153 "grandfather") and can reorder lines (II 59/60). Identity of the Akkadian, not of the English, governs the Hebrew.
- **Reserve letters, not only roots,** for a key comparison word: `לִקְרַאת` (root קרה) would have put the letters of `קרא` in a line next to the *nabû* line; prefer a word that cannot be misread as the reserved one.
- **An echo can come from the corpus's own renderings**: Job 26:12 `רָגַע הַיָּם` matches II 150 only because pašāḫu Š was rendered `הִרְגִּיעַ`. Such links are recorded as [equivalent] with an explicit note that the Hebrew, not the Akkadian, carries the shared root.

## Tablet III

### Scope
Tablet III, eBL lines 1–138 (eBL has no section breaks and no old line numbers for this tablet; the Hebrew headings are ours):
- **1–12** Anšar speaks to his vizier Kakka (`כַּכָּא`): go to Laḫmu and Laḫamu, have all the gods brought; let them converse, sit at a banquet, eat grain and drink beer, and decree destiny for Marduk; repeat my words to them.
- **13–66** Anšar's message as Kakka is to deliver it: Ea's report of Tiāmat's assembly (15–18 = II 11–14), the fixed passage of her army and Qingu (19–52 = I 129–162), Anu and Nudimmud turned back (53–54), Marduk's readiness (55–57) and his conditions (58–64 = II 156–162), "hurry, decree him your destiny, let him confront your strong enemy" (65–66).
- **67–70** Kakka's journey; he prostrates himself before Laḫmu and Laḫamu and stands up to speak.
- **71–124** Kakka repeats the message word for word (71–76 = 13–18; 77–110 = 19–52; 111–124 = 53–66).
- **125–128** Laḫmu, Laḫamu and the Igigi wail: "what has turned hostile … we do not know Tiāmat's deed".
- **129–138** the great gods stream to Anšar, rejoice, kiss, converse and sit at the banquet (133–134 = 8–9), eat, drink beer until bloated and carefree, and decree destiny for Marduk (138 = 10).

Files: `annotated-III.rtl.md` (138 entries), `translation-III.rtl.md` (introduction, 10 section headings, 138 lines, 12 footnotes).

### Sources consulted
- eBL `L/1/2` SB III: `reading-edition.md` (N, variants, notes, parallels, the ATF of all 23 manuscripts for every non-repeated line and for every broken line of the repeated ones), `glossary.md` (stems), `manuscripts.md`, `_raw/chapter-display.json` (checked: no `isBeginningOfSection`, no `oldLineNumbers`).
- eBL English (Foster) as rank-3 comparison, extracted separately and read only after the Hebrew of all 138 lines was drafted.
- King 1902 vol. 1 (history only: "Gaga", the banquet 8–9 and 133–137 – "bread", "mix wine", "sesame-wine", "drunk", "wholly at ease").
- `annotated-I.rtl.md` (I 31, I 35–36, I 129–162), `annotated-II.rtl.md` (II 11–14, 15–48, 155–162) and both tablets' project sections; the corpus and project renderings; the flood corpus renderings.
- The repository's vocalized Bible via `tools/check_bible_quotes.py --show`.
- Not used: Lambert 2013, Talon, Kämmerer & Metzler (paid); Langdon 1923 (not needed).

### Decisions
1. **Repeated lines** copied character for character from their first occurrence: III 3 = I 31 with `כַּכָּא` for `מומו`; 15–18 / 73–76 = II 11–14 with `[לאמור]:` / `לאמור:` (*umma*) for `אבי,` in 15/73; 19–52 / 77–110 = the fixed passage (`renderings.md` §2, spliced by script with eBL's N); 58–64 / 116–122 = II 156–162; 71–124 = 13–66; 133–134, 138 = 8–10 with the tense changed (precative → preterite / present).
2. **Brackets** (the prompt's rule, which supersedes coordinator decision 4b for this tablet): a Hebrew word is bracketed when its Akkadian root is wholly or largely restored in eBL's N in *all* manuscripts of this tablet, even when the parallel line preserves it; a word of which only an ending (case, plural, suffix) is restored is not bracketed (the פירוק says so). A word preserved in a variant manuscript is not bracketed (110: eBL brackets the whole main reconstruction, but NinNA1 preserves *imtuk ina kitmuru mag[šari]*). Bracketed copies are listed below ("Lines shared with other tablets").
3. **Vocalization in repeated lines:** names are vocalized at their first occurrence in III; where a repeated pair inside III contains a first occurrence (15/73 `תִּיאָמַת`, 53/111 `אָנוּ`, 54/112 `נוּדִימּוּד`), both copies are vocalized, so that the pair stays identical. The fixed passage keeps its own vocalization (`חֻבּוּר` 23, `קִינְגּוּ` 38 are first occurrences; `אנו` 49 and `האנונכי` 46 stay unvocalized there).
4. **Kakka** = `כַּכָּא` (since the 2026-10-23 harmonization; drafted as `גַּגָּא`, corpus §1, open decision 1 of the coordinator). Note in III 2: eBL *Kakka*, the sign *ka₃* = GA; possible play on *kakku* "weapon" (24, 34).
5. **The banquet vocabulary:** *qerītu* = `סְעוּדָה` (not `מִשְׁתֶּה`, which would echo *šatû*); *lišāna šakānu* = `שָׂם לָשׁוֹן (בשיחה)` (image kept, gloss in parentheses); *ašnan* = `דָּגָן` [equivalent]; *akālu* = `אָכַל` [cognate]; ***patāqu* II "to drink" = `גָּמָא`** (homonym of *patāqu* I "to shape" = `עִצֵּב`, 23/81; kept apart from *šatû* = `שָׁתָה`, 136); *kurunnu* transliterated `כּוּרוּן`; *šikaru* = `שֵׁכָר` [cognate] (flood corpus); *arsu* = `גְּרִיסִים`; *sanānu* D = `*גָּדַשׁ*` (uncertain; the ancient commentary glosses *malû*); *rāṭu* = `צִנּוֹר`; *ḫabāṣu* G stative = `נָפוּחַ` (Foster "felt good" follows the D/Š sense).
6. **There is no *akalu* "bread" in Tablet III.** The food word is *ašnan* "grain"; "bread" (King 1902, and the coordinator's note) goes back to older readings. The *akalu* = `לֶחֶם` rendering is therefore not used here; it stays available for later tablets.
7. **The gods' "relaxation" (137) is *egû* "to be careless, negligent"**, not *nâḫu* or *pašāḫu*: rendered `שַׁאֲנַן` (Job 3:18; Amos 6:1), so that no rest root (`נוח`, `רגע`, `שבת`) appears; `רָפָה` is avoided because it renders *rabābu* in the fixed passage (52).
8. ***(w)âru* D "to commission" = `צִוָּה`** (13, 71), and *têrtu* = `צַו` (14, 72): the Hebrew keeps the Akkadian root link (*têrtu* is from *wâru*). *âru* G stays `נִגַּשׁ` (11, 55, 56).
9. ***ṣabāru* Gt "to speak" / Š "to make speak" = root `מלל`** (5 `לְמַלֵּל`, 14/72 `נתן לי לְמַלֵּל`), kept apart from *zakāru* `דִּבֵּר` and *atmû* `שָׂח` (57/115).
10. ***nakru* "enemy" = `יָרִיב`** (66/124), because `אוֹיֵב` = *lemnu* and `צַר` = *gērû*; ***nakāru* G = `הִתְנַכֵּר`** (127) [cognate נכר], Dt `הוּמַר` (63, fixed from II); ***dannu* = `חָזָק`** (`עַז` is *nadru* in the fixed passage).
11. **Haste:** *ḫamāṭu* = `חָשׁ` (65/123 `חושו`), *arḫiš* = `מְהֵרָה`. (II has *aruḫ* `מהר` and *urruḫiš* `חיש`, both from *araḫu*; the overlap of roots cannot be avoided completely. Recorded as a proposal.)
12. **Other new terms:** *šeʾû* Gtn infinitive `לַחְקֹר` (5, as II *šeʾû* `חָקַר`); *leʾû* = `יָכֹל` (5, 53); *abāku* G `הֵבִיא` (Š imperative `הָבֵא`, 6–7); *nagbu* "totality" = `כֻּלָּם` (7; homonym of *nagbu* "underground water", noted); *ašriš* = `אֶל מְקוֹם` (4, 68); *qudmu* = `לִפְנֵי` (11; cognate קדם noted); *umma* = `לֵאמֹר` (15, 73); *šukênu* = `הִשְׁתַּחֲוָה` (69; without `אפו ארצה`, which is *labān appi*); *ešēru* G = `הִתְיַשֵּׁר` (70) [cognate]; *redû* Š = `שָׂם פְּעָמָיו` (67, as II 8); *garāšu* N = `נָהַר` (129); *napḫaru* + suffix = `כֻּלָּם` (126); *nâqu* = `הֵילִיל`; *marṣiš* = `בִּמְרִירוּת`; *ṣibit ṭēmi* = `אֲחִיזַת עֵצָה` (127, *…*); *ḫidûtu* = `שִׂמְחָה`; *našāqu* N = `הִתְנַשֵּׁק`; *aḫu u aḫu* = `אָח וָאָח`; *elīta* = `בְּקוֹל רָם` (as I 36).
13. **Variants recorded:** the Assyrian manuscript AššNA1 reads *ana anšar* for *ana marduk* in 10 and 138 (the Assyrian recension's Anšar = Aššur); III 40 NinNA1 and III 98 BabaNB3 have *tebû* for *dekû* (`קימת המדון`); 44 NinNA2 *qātuššu*; 49 BabaNB2 *enūti*; 69 BabaNB3 *šapalšun*; 70 BabaNB3 *ikmis izzaz-ma*; 6 NinNA1 *ana maḫrīka*; 8 *lušbū* in two manuscripts; BabaNB1 omits 16–51 and 77–123 (it jumps from 15 to 52 and from 76 to 124).
14. **eBL's English differs** in 9 / 134 ("produce of the field … the vine": the Akkadian has grain and *kurunnu*-beer), 57 / 115 (*epšu pîšu* is the object, not a temporal clause), 136 ("felt good" vs the G stative "distended"), 132 (the restoration "of the gods" is Foster's).

### Open decisions for the user
1. **Kakka / Gaga**: `גַּגָּא` kept (coordinator's open decision 1). Further thought: eBL's normalization *kaka* reflects the sign values *ka₃-ka₃*, which can equally be read *ga-ga*; Lambert and George read *Kakka*. A neutral Hebrew `כַּכָּא` would follow eBL without the nursery sound of `קַקָּה`. (Adopted 2026-10-23.)
2. **Brackets in the fixed passage of III** (decision 2): 18 lines of the two copies carry brackets that Tablets I–II do not have. This follows this tablet's instructions and is allowed by `verify.py`, but it reverses coordinator decision 4b for Tablet III. Alternative: drop them, as in I and II.
3. **9 / 134 *kurunnu***: transliterated `כּוּרוּן`. Alternatives: `מִמְסָךְ` (Isa 65:11, a drink poured for a god), or `שֵׁכָר` (but that is *šikaru*, 136).
4. **137 *egû* = `שַׁאֲנָנוּ`**: carries both "at ease" and "complacent". Alternatives: `הִתְרַשְּׁלוּ` (literal, but flat), `הִתְעַצְּלוּ`.
5. **127** is a crux: `מה התנכר, עד אשר קנתה *אחיזת [עצה]* עלינו?` (with Foster: "what has gone wrong, that she has taken action against us?"). Alternative literal reading: "what has changed, so that (she) has taken hold of our plan?"
6. **135 *usanninū***: `*גדשו*` (after the ancient commentary's *malû*); alternative `*הזרימו*` (eBL dictionary: "make flow in a channel?").
7. **8 / 133 *lišāna šakānu***: `ישימו לשון (בשיחה)` keeps the Akkadian image; alternative `ישוחחו` (but `שָׂח` is reserved for *atmû*).
8. **66 *nakru* = `יָרִיב`** (decision 10); alternative `שׂוֹנֵא` (but *zêru* is `שׂנא`, 15).

Echo grep of all `>` lines for `תהום רקיע תנין ברא הבדיל שבת צבא מאור אלוהים יצר כבש רדה ברך קרא נח מלאכ צלם דמות`: only `בראש` (fixed passage 39/97) and `קִרְאוּ` (60/118, *nabû*, intended).
- 2026-10-16: `translation-III.rtl.md` generated from the annotated headings and `>` lines (script), with an introduction and 12 footnotes. King 1902 consulted for the history of the banquet lines (added to the apparatus of 9, 136, 137).
- 2026-10-16, **verification**: `python3 -I verify.py III` → "Tablet III: 138 annotated entries (eBL 138), 138 translation lines; fixed passage: 34 rows checked, 0 mismatch(es); 6 difference(s) in repeated lines; problems: 0". The 6 differences are all justified by the Akkadian: I 31 / III 3 (the name), II 11 / III 15 and II 11 / III 73 (*umma* for *abī*; plus the vocalized name), III 8 / 133, 9 / 134, 10 / 138 (precative vs preterite / present). 76 further pairs (III with I, II and III) differ in brackets only (the broken copies listed below), and the fixed passage reports 18 bracket-only copies (allowed). `check_bible_quotes.py` on `annotated-III.rtl.md`, `translation-III.rtl.md` and this file → 0 problems.

| 128 | *ša tiāmti epištaš* | מעשה תיאמת | `וַיִּשְׁבֹּת בַּיּוֹם הַשְּׁבִיעִי מִכָּל מְלַאכְתּוֹ אֲשֶׁר עָשָׂה` (בראשית ב:ב) | [equivalent?] | *epištu* (from *epēšu*, `עשה`) "deed, work": the chaos mother's "work" is war; God's `מְלָאכָה` is creation. |
| 132 | *innašqū aḫu u aḫu* | התנשקו אח ואח | `וַיְנַשֵּׁק לְכָל אֶחָיו` (בראשית מה:טו) | [cognate] (*našāqu* = נשק) / [equivalent] | Brothers kissing in reconciliation and joy. |
| 136 | *šikra ina šatê ḫabṣū zumra* | בשתות שכר – נפוחי גוף | `וַיֵּשְׁתְּ מִן הַיַּיִן וַיִּשְׁכָּר` (בראשית ט:כא); `וַיִּשְׁתּוּ וַיִּשְׁכְּרוּ עִמּוֹ` (בראשית מג:לד) | [cognate] (*šikaru* = שכר, *šatû* = שתה) / [divergence] | The gods drink themselves bloated; in Genesis drunkenness is human (Noah, Joseph's brothers). |
| 137 | *maʾdiš egû kabattašun ītelṣū* | מאוד שאננו, כבדם עלץ | `וַיִּשְׁבֹּת בַּיּוֹם הַשְּׁבִיעִי מִכָּל מְלַאכְתּוֹ אֲשֶׁר עָשָׂה` (בראשית ב:ב); `תִּירוֹשִׁי הַמְשַׂמֵּחַ אֱלֹהִים וַאֲנָשִׁים` (שופטים ט:יג); `עָלַץ לִבִּי בַּיהוָה` (שמואל א ב:א) | [divergence] / [equivalent]; [cognate] (*elēṣu* = עלץ) | The gods' ease is *egû* "carelessness" after drinking, before the war – not *nâḫu* / *pašāḫu*, and not rest after completed work. |
| 129 | *iggaršūnim-ma illakūni* | נהרו ובאו | `וְנָהֲרוּ אֵלָיו כָּל הַגּוֹיִם` (ישעיהו ב:ב) | [equivalent] | The streaming of many to the divine king's seat (the shared root is the translation's, not the Akkadian's). |

### Lines shared with other tablets
- III 3 ~ I 31 (name only: `כַּכָּא` / `מומו`).
- III 15–18 = II 11–14 (15: *umma* for *abī*); III 73–76 = 15–18.
- III 19–52 = III 77–110 = I 129–162 = II 15–48 (the fixed passage). **Bracketed copies** (this tablet's breaks): 36, 37, 38, 40, 41, 42, 43, 44, 45, 49; 91, 98, 105, 106, 107, 108, 109, 110.
- III 58–64 = III 116–122 = II 156–162 (116–122 bracketed in their second halves).
- Internal: 13–14 = 71–72 (14 bracketed); 53–57 = 111–115 and 65–66 = 123–124 (111–115, 123 bracketed); 8–10 ~ 133–134, 138 (tense).
- **Hebrew of lines that later tablets may repeat:** IV opens with the gods' answer; the banquet lines (8–9 / 133–134) and 10 / 138 are the forms to reuse if Marduk's destiny or the banquet recur: `ישימו לשון (בשיחה), בסעודה יֵשְׁבוּ,` / `דָּגָן יאכלו, יִגְמְאוּ כּוּרוּן,` / `לְמַרְדּוּךְ, המשיב גמולם, יגזרו גורל!` (and past forms `שמו …`, `דגן אכלו, גָּמְאוּ כורון,`, `למרדוך, המשיב גמולם, גוזרים גורל.`).

### Proposals for the shared renderings
For the corpus `renderings.md` (§3) or the project §4 (rows added there):
- ***egû* "to be careless" = `שַׁאֲנַן`** – a fourth "repose" word, kept apart from *nâḫu* / *pašāḫu* / *napāšu*; add a note to the rest-vocabulary row of corpus §3 that the banquet's ease (III 137) is *egû*.
- ***patāqu* II "to drink" = `גָּמָא`**, ***šatû* = `שָׁתָה`**, ***šikaru* = `שֵׁכָר`** (flood corpus), ***qerītu* = `סְעוּדָה`**, ***ašnan* = `דָּגָן`**, ***akālu* = `אָכַל`** [cognate]; reserve *akalu* = `לֶחֶם` [equivalent] (with *akalu* ~ `אֹכֶל` noted) for its first real occurrence (VI–VII?).
- ***nakru* = `יָרִיב`; *nakāru* G = `הִתְנַכֵּר`** [cognate]; ***dannu* = `חָזָק`**.
- ***(w)âru* D = `צִוָּה`** (with *têrtu* = `צַו`); *âru* G stays `נִגַּשׁ`.
- ***ṣabāru* Gt/Š = `מִלֵּל`**.
- ***šukênu* = `הִשְׁתַּחֲוָה`** (without `אפו ארצה`, which stays for *labān appi*).
- **Haste:** *ḫamāṭu* = `חָשׁ`, *arḫiš* = `מְהֵרָה`; the corpus may want to align *araḫu* / *urruḫiš* (II: `מהר` / `חיש`) in IV–VII.
- **Kakka**: see open decision 1.

### Lessons for the guidelines
- **A repeated passage can be the best-preserved or the worst-preserved copy.** When a later copy is broken and the earlier one intact, keep the brackets of the broken copy (verify by "brackets-only" comparison), and state the bracket rule once in the file header; otherwise each translator invents a different threshold (I 32 `עצ[ה]`, II 73 `הולמ[ים]`, III whole words only).
- **Vocalize a name in both members of an internal repeated pair** when the first member is the name's first occurrence; otherwise the pair differs in niqqud only and the verification script reports a false difference.
- **Check the coordinator's expected vocabulary against the edition before drafting**: the prompt expected *akalu* "bread" in III 129–138, but the text has *ašnan* "grain"; the "bread" goes back to King 1902's reading of the same lines.
- **Homonyms inside one poem need separate Hebrew** (*patāqu* I "shape" / II "drink"; *nagbu* "totality" / "underground water"), and the apparatus should name the other homonym, since ancient commentaries (NinNACom1) already played on them.

## Tablet IV

### Scope
Tablet IV, eBL lines 1–146 (eBL has no section breaks and no old line numbers for this tablet; the Hebrew headings are ours):
- **1–18** the gods set up a princely dais; their acclamation: "you are honoured among the great gods … your command is Anu" (3–4 = 5–6), "to raise and to bring low is your hand", "you are our avenger" (13 ~ II 127, 156), kingship over everything; mercy to the god who trusts, death to the god who takes up evil.
- **19–26** the test: a constellation (*lumāšu*) is set up; "destroy and build – command, and let them be firm" (22); at his word it is destroyed and built again (25–26).
- **27–34** the gods rejoice and bless (*karābu*): "Marduk is king"; sceptre, throne, *palû*, an irresistible weapon; "go, cut Tiāmat's throat; let the winds carry her blood as good news" (32 ~ 132).
- **35–58** the weapons: bow, arrow and bowstring, mace, quiver, lightning, flame, the net, the four winds (Anu's gift), seven winds he builds, the Deluge (49 = 75), the storm chariot with four named horses, Battle and Strife at his sides, armour of terror and radiance.
- **59–70** the march; Marduk inspects Tiāmat and Qingu; Qingu and his gods are confused.
- **71–92** Tiāmat's spell and lie (73–74 broken at the beginning); Marduk raises the Deluge and challenges her ("why have you risen … you named Qingu your consort … let us fight single combat"); Tiāmat goes mad, shrieks, casts spells.
- **93–104** the duel: the net, the evil wind into her open mouth, the belly distended, the arrow splits the belly, cuts the heart; he binds her, extinguishes her life, stands on her corpse.
- **105–122** her forces scattered, her gods netted and imprisoned; the eleven creatures (*ištēn-ešret nabnīti*) roped and trampled; Qingu bound, counted with the dead gods (Uggu); the Tablet of Destinies taken and sealed.
- **123–134** (123 = I 73; 125 ~ I 74) Marduk returns to Tiāmat, crushes her skull, severs her veins, the north wind carries her blood as good news; the fathers rejoice and send gifts.
- **135–146** the lord rests, inspects the corpse, divides the *šerkuppu* and builds artful things; he splits her like a fish for drying; half of her he sets up as a roof for the heavens, stretches the skin, posts guards ordered not to let her waters out; crosses the heavens, sets them opposite the Apsû, measures the Apsû and founds Ešgalla/Ešarra as its replica; Anu, Enlil and Ea are settled in their cult-places.

Files: `annotated-IV.rtl.md` (146 entries), `translation-IV.rtl.md` (introduction, 10 section headings, 146 lines, 26 footnotes).

### Sources consulted
- eBL `L/1/2` SB IV: `reading-edition.md` (N, variants, notes, parallels; the ATF of 11, 70–76, 116, 120, 124, 136), `glossary.md` (stems), `manuscripts.md` (40 manuscripts), `_raw/chapter-display.json` (checked: no `isBeginningOfSection`, no `oldLineNumbers`).
- eBL English (Foster) as rank-3 comparison, extracted to a separate file and read only after the Hebrew of all 146 lines was drafted.
- King 1902 vol. 1 (history only: "garment" for *lumāšu* in 19–26, "thunderbolt" for *abūbu* in 49, "flesh of the …" in 136, "flat fish" in 137, "bolt" for *mašku* in 139).
- The annotated files and project sections of Tablets I–III; corpus and project renderings; the flood corpus renderings.
- The repository's vocalized Bible via `tools/check_bible_quotes.py --show`.
- Not used: Lambert 2013, Talon 2005, Kämmerer & Metzler 2012 (paid); Langdon 1923 (not needed: the eBL text is nearly complete).

### Decisions
1. **Repeated lines**: IV 123 = I 73 copied character for character (`אחרי אשר את אויביו אסר, הכה,`); IV 125 follows the frame of I 74 (`העמיד את נצחון … על …`) and changes only what the Akkadian changes (*ernitti anšar*, *nakirī*, *kalîš*); 3–4 = 5–6 (only the vocative differs; Anu vocalized in both 4 and 6); 49 = 75 (75 `[נשא]` bracketed, the verb is broken in all manuscripts); 69 / 107 differ only in the pronoun (*-šu* / *-ša*); 13 *mutirru gimillīni* = `המשיב גמולנו` (after II 127, 156; III 10); 32 / 132 share `לבשורה` and *wabālu* `יבל`; 59b = III 67b (`לאורחו שם פעמיו`); 122 echoes the fixed passage 157 (`הצמיד … אל חזהו`).
2. **IV 19–26, creation by word**: *lumāšu* = `מַזָּל`; *abātu* = `הָרַס`, *banû* = `בָּנָה` (N `נִבְנָה`), *qabû* = `אָמַר`, ***kânu* Gt *liktūnā* = `וְיִכּוֹנוּ`** (the root of `וַיְהִי כֵן`); 23 *epšu pîka* kept as a nominative heading (`מעשה פיך – ייהרס המזל!`), no added preposition.
3. ***karābu* = `בֵּרֵךְ`** (28), by meaning (the natural Hebrew for "bless, greet, pray"), tagged [equivalent] with Gen 1:22, 28 `וַיְבָרֶךְ`; the apparatus states that *karābu* is not cognate of ברך (it is the source of `כְּרוּב`).
4. **The weapons**: *qaštu* `קֶשֶׁת` [cognate]; *mulmullu* `חֵץ`; *rakābu* Š `הִרְכִּיב`; *kânu* D `כּוֹנֵן` + *matnu* `יֶתֶר` (= Ps 11:2 `כּוֹנְנוּ חִצָּם עַל-יֶתֶר`); *miṭṭu* `אַלָּה` (not `מַטֶּה`, which would import Ex 14:16); *išpatu* `אַשְׁפָּה` [cognate]; *berqu* `בָּרָק` [cognate]; *nablu* `לֶהָבָה`; *sapāru* `רֶשֶׁת`; *imḫullu* `רוּחַ־רָעָה` (hyphenated; the gloss *šāra lemna* set off by dashes); *meḫû* `סוּפָה`, *ašamšūtu* `סוּפַת־אָבָק`, *imsuḫḫu* `עַלְעוֹל`; *abūbu* `מַבּוּל` with the note that here it is a weapon; *narkabtu* `מֶרְכָּבָה` [cognate]; *naṣmadu* `צֶמֶד` [cognate]; *naḫlaptu* `חֲלִיפָה` [cognate]; *šammu* `סַם` [cognate].
5. **The four winds** (43) by meaning: `דָּרוֹם, צָפוֹן, קָדִים, מַעֲרָב`; the images (*šadû* "mountain", *amurru* "Amurru") in the note.
6. ***wabālu* = `הוֹבִיל`** (cognate יבל; 32, 132, 134), so that `נָשָׂא` stays for *našû*. (III 56 had `נשא` for *wabālu* in the idiom "his heart carried him"; noted as a proposal.)
7. ***ṣabātu*: G `תָּפַס` (96), Š/Št `הִצִּיב`** (34, 42, 139: "posted, stationed"); *aḫāzu* stays `אָחַז` (18, 37 Š `הֶאֱחִיז`).
8. **The duel**: *karšu* `בֶּטֶן` (Tablet I), with `כָּרֵשׂ` and Jer 51:34 in the note; *ṣânu* `טָעַן` ("load, fill till bloated"; also 115 `טעונים אימות`) – not `נִפַּח` (= *napāḫu*); *esēlu* N `צָבָה` (Num 5:27); *nepelkû* Š `פָּעַר`; *ḫepû* `בִּקַּע` (101, 137); *batāqu* D `בִּתֵּק` [cognate]; *šalāṭu* `פִּלַּח`; *la'ātu* `בָּלַע`; *belû* D `כִּבָּה`; *šalamtu* `גְּוִיָּה`.
9. **Five enemy words kept apart**: *lemnu* `אוֹיֵב` (fixed), *gērû* `צַר`, *nakru* `יָרִיב`, *zayyāru* `שׂוֹנֵא` (from *zêru* = `שָׂנֵא`, III 15), ***ayyābu* `קָם`** (124) – although *ayyābu* is the cognate of `אוֹיֵב`, which is fixed for *lemnu* in the line just before (123 = I 73); the cognate is stated in the note.
10. ***parāru* Dt (106) = `פּוֹרְרוּ`** (cognate; Ps 74:13 `פוֹרַרְתָּ … יָם`); *uptarrira* parsed as Dt (passive, like the parallel N *issapḫa*), as eBL translates.
11. ***kamāru* (112) = `מִכְמֹרֶת`** [cognate] (Hab 1:15), following eBL's lemma "in the trap"; eBL's English follows *kamāru* "heap".
12. **The sky (135–140)** per corpus renderings §3: *nâḫu* `נָח` (135, the only `נח` of the tablet), *zâzu* D `חִלֵּק`, *nikiltu* `מְחֻכָּמוֹת` (root link with *nakālu* D `חִכֵּם`, I 62, 93), *ḫepû* `בִּקַּע`, *nūnu* `דָּג`, *mašṭû* `מִשְׁטָח` (Ezek 26:5; 47:10), *mišlu* `מַחֲצִית`, *ṣullulu* `קֵרָה`, *šadādu* `מָתַח`, *mašku* `עוֹר`, *maṣṣaru* `נוֹצֵר`, *(w)âru* D `צִוָּה`. **`רָקִיעַ` does not occur.**
13. **136 *šerkuppu*** = `*הַמְּצוּלָה*` (italic: uncertain translation), following eBL's note (*Malku* II 37 *serkuppu* = *tâmtu* "sea") and Foster's "brine"; the old reading "(flesh of) the foetus" and the lemma gloss "reed marsh" are in the note.
14. **141 *ašratu* = `זְבוּל`** (a poetic word for heaven as the gods' seat, 1 Kgs 8:13; Isa 63:15). eBL's English "firmament" is recorded as a false echo (Vulgate *firmamentum* = `רָקִיעַ`). *ebēru* = `עָבַר` [cognate], hence *etēqu* (10) = `הִסִּיג` (Deut 19:14).
15. **142–146**: *meḫret* `לְעֻמַּת` (Eccl 7:14); *maḫāru* Št `הִשְׁוָה`; *mašāḫu* `מָדַד` (cognate of Aramaic משח noted); *binûtu* `מִבְנֶה`; *tamšīlu* `תַּבְנִית`; Ešgalla `אֵשְׁגַלָּה`, Ešarra `אֵשַׁרָּה`; *māḫāzu* `מָחוֹז`; *ramû* G `הִתְיַשֵּׁב` (2), Š `יִשֵּׁב` (146) – the same Hebrew root as *ašābu* `יָשַׁב` (15, 112); noted.
16. **Other new terms**: *rubûtu* `נְסִיכוּת`; *nadû* (a dais) `עָרַךְ` (1; elsewhere `הֵטִיל`); *sāgu* `אוּלָם`; *zanānūtu* `כַּלְכָּלָה`; *qibītu* `אֲמִירָה`; *šanānu* (*lā šanān*) `אֵין מִתְחָרֶה`; *sarāru*/*sartu* `כָּזָב`; *lullû* IV `שֶׁקֶר(?)`; *napalṭû* N `הֶחֱטִיא`; *râsu* D `נִפֵּץ`; *gamālu* `חָמַל` (cognate גמל noted); *tabāku* `יָצַק` (flood corpus); *parā'u* `גָּדַע`; *da'āpu* `הָדַף`; *palû* `תְּקוּפַת מְלוּכָה`; *ḫaṭṭu* `שֵׁבֶט`; *tešmû* `הַקְשָׁבָה`; *rēṣu* `עוֹזֵר`; *ašarēdu* `בָּכִיר`; *šagāšu* `קָטַל`; *raḫāṣu* `שָׁטַף`; *mupparšu* `מְעוֹפֵף`; *sapānu* `רָמַס`; *rašbu* `אָיֹם`, *rašubbu* `מַבְעִית`, *galtu* `נוֹרָא`; *ḫalāpu* stat. `עָטוּי`; *kullu* `הֶחֱזִיק`; *tamāḫu* G `תָּמַךְ` (62) but `הִצְמִיד` in 122 (echo of I 157); *ešû* `נָבוֹךְ` (67, 70); *seḫû* `מְשֻׁבָּשׁ`; *barû* `בָּחַן`; *ṭeḫû* `קָרַב` (as II); *qerēbu* Št `הִקְרִיב`, Gt `הִתְקָרֵב`; *dâṣu* `בִּזָּה`; *rēmu* `רַחֲמִים` [cognate]; *rakāsu* `חָגַר`; *šašmu* `דּוּ־קְרָב`; *maḫḫûtu* `מְשֻׁגַּעַת` (not `מִתְנַבְּאָה`, to keep the root of *nabû*/נביא out); *šanû ṭēmu* `שִׁנָּה טַעַם` (1 Sam 21:14); *manû* `שִׁנֵּן` (91, "recite"), `מָנָה` (120, "count"); *šêlu* `לָטַשׁ` (Ps 7:13); *elēpu* Gt `נִשְׂתָּרֵג`; *šuparruru* `פָּרַשׂ`; *wuššuru* `שִׁלַּח`; *esēru* `עָצַר` (cognate אסר noted; `אָסַר` = *kamû*); *šebēru* `שִׁבֵּר`; *dumāmu* `אֲנָקָה`; *šērtu* `עֹנֶשׁ`; *kalû* `כָּלָא`; *kišukku* `שֶׁבִי`; *mīlu gallê* `גֵּאוּת־שֵׁדִים`; *kartappu* `*נוֹהֵג*`; *kasû* D `כָּפַת`; *Uggu* `אֵלֵי הַמָּוֶת(?)`; *ekēmu* `נָטַל`; *kanāku* `חָתַם`, *kišibbu* `חוֹתָם`; *nizmatu* `חֵפֶץ`; *ṣibittu* `תְּפִיסָה`; *letû* D `רִסֵּק`; *ušultu* `עוֹרֵק`; *riāšu* `שָׂשׂ`; *igisû* `תְּשׁוּרָה`; *šulmānu* `שַׁלְמֹנִים` [cognate].
17. **eBL's English departs from its own normalization** in 44 (the net, not the winds, as Anu's gift), 51 ("his side" for *idušša*), 76–77 (*igmilu* "conciliatory", *ṭubbātu* "friendliness" against the normalized *ikmilu*, *tubbâti* < *tebû*), 85 (concessive *lū*), 144 ("the likeness of Esharra"), 145 (locatives). The Hebrew translates the normalization; each case is in the apparatus.

### Open decisions for the user
1. **136 *šerkuppu***: `*המצולה*` (a poetic "sea", after eBL's *Malku* note). Alternatives: a transliteration `סֶרְכֻּפּוּ` with the note; "reed marsh" (`*סְבַךְ הַקָּנִים*`, the dictionary lemma, which would strengthen the link to Ps 136:13 `יַם-סוּף`); the old "foetus".
2. **145** syntax: "Ešarra, which he built (as) the heavens" vs. eBL's three locatives (Ešgalla, Ešarra, heaven) for 146. The Hebrew `אשגלה, אשרה אשר בנה, השמים –` keeps both open.
3. **77 / 76**: we translate the normalization ("why have you risen, lifted on high … who was angry"); eBL's English reads *ṭubbātu* / *igmilu* ("why do you put on a friendly face … who acted conciliatory"). This changes the scene (open hostility vs. feigned friendliness).
4. **28 *karābu* = `בֵּרְכוּ`**: by meaning, with a [equivalent] tag to Gen 1:22. Alternative `הֵרִיעוּ` / `בֵּרְכוּ לְשָׁלוֹם` would avoid any contact with `וַיְבָרֶךְ`, but would hide a real conceptual parallel (blessing by the gods of their new king vs. God's blessing of his creatures).
5. **124 *ayyābu* = `הַקָּם`** instead of the cognate `אוֹיֵב` (decision 9). Alternative: give `אוֹיֵב` to *ayyābu* and change *lemnu* to `רָשָׁע`/`מֵרֵעַ` everywhere (would change I 73 = IV 123).
6. **29 *palû***: `תקופת מלוכה` (eBL lemma) or "staff (of office)" (Foster), i.e. `מַטֵּה הַשִּׁלְטוֹן`.
7. **120 *Uggu***: `אֵלֵי הַמָּוֶת(?)` (by meaning) or the transliteration `אוּגּוּ`.
8. **2 / 146 *ramû***: `התיישב` / `ישב` share the Hebrew root of *ašābu*; alternative `שָׁכַן` / `הִשְׁכִּין` for *ramû* (keeps the verbs apart, but `לשלטון שכן` is awkward).
9. **62 / 122 *tamāḫu***: G `תמוך` in 62, but `הצמיד` in 122 to echo I 157 (Š `הצמידה`). Alternative: `תמך` in both (strict lemma consistency, the echo lost).
10. **85 *lū***: precative ("let your army be harnessed!") or concessive (eBL: "though your main force is drawn up").

Changed: 23 `במעשה פיך` → `מעשה פיך –` (the Akkadian has a nominative, no preposition); 37 `(בה)` marked as added; 97 `לבולעו` → `לבלוע` (no object in the Akkadian); 44, 109, 117, 132, 138, 142 additions in ( ) checked. Brackets: only 71 `[לחשה]` and 75 `[נשא]` (roots broken in all manuscripts); 76 *[a]na*, *išpurš[i]* (one sign / an ending) not bracketed; 73–74 `…` for the lost beginnings. `?` for 70, 72, 73, 74, 120, 124; `*…*` for 116, 136.

The function is shared with Gen 1:6–8: a barrier that keeps the upper waters from coming down. Hebrew רקע "to beat out (metal) into a thin sheet" (Ex 39:3; Num 17:3 `רִקֻּעֵי פַחִים`; Job 37:18 the sky "beaten out" "hard as a cast mirror"; Isa 42:5, Ps 136:6 of the earth) **adds** a solid, hammered surface made by a craftsman-god's word, and **removes** the corpse, the skin, the guards and the enemy: in Gen 1 the barrier needs no watchmen. The "stretching" of the heavens like a tent or curtain (נטה, מתח: Isa 40:22; Ps 104:2) is the biblical image closest to *šadādu maška*; Ps 104:3 `הַמְקָרֶה` is the closest to *ṣullulu*. The Hebrew translation never uses רָקִיעַ. |

No naming (*nabû*) of the sky here. |
