### Lines shared with other tablets
- IV 123 = I 73 (exact).
- IV 125 ~ I 74 (frame kept; *ernitti anšar*, *nakirī*, *kalîš* differ).
- IV 13 ~ II 127, II 156, III 10 (*mutīr gimilli*).
- IV 59b = III 67b (*urḫašu ušardī-ma*).
- IV 122 ~ I 157 = II 43 = III 47 = III 105 (*irtuš itmuḫ* / *iratuš ušatmiḫ*).
- IV 37 ~ V 95 (eBL); IV 111 ~ V 74; IV 138 ~ V 62 (for the Tablet V translator).
- Internal: 3 = 5 (vocative), 4 = 6, 49 = 75, 69 ~ 107 (pronoun), 32 ~ 132, 31 ~ 131 (*parā'u* G / D), 42 ~ 140 (*lā aṣê* / *lā šūṣâ*), 27–28 ~ 133.
- **Hebrew of lines that later tablets may repeat or echo**: 37 `נשא את האַלָּה, האחיז (בה) את ימינו;` (V 95); 111 `עצר אותם, ואת נשקיהם שיבר;` (V 74); 115 `ואחד־עשר הַמִּבְנִים, אשר טעונים אימות,` (V 73); 138 `את מחציתה שם, וקֵרָה (בה) את השמים;` (V 62); 141–146 (Ešarra, the Apsû, the cult-places) for V–VI.

### Proposals for the shared renderings
For the corpus `renderings.md` (§3) or the project §4 (rows added there):
- ***karābu* = `בֵּרֵךְ`** [equivalent] (not cognate; source of `כְּרוּב`), so that later blessings (VI–VII) align with IV 28.
- ***wabālu* = `הוֹבִיל`** [cognate יבל] corpus-wide; `נָשָׂא` for *našû* only. (III 56 `לבו נשא (אותו)` could become `לבו הוביל (אותו)`; the coordinator decides.)
- ***ṣabātu* G = `תָּפַס`, Š/Št = `הִצִּיב`**; *aḫāzu* = `אָחַז`.
- **Enemy words**: add *zayyāru* = `שׂוֹנֵא` and *ayyābu* = `קָם` (cognate of `אוֹיֵב`, noted) to the row of *lemnu*/*gērû*/*nakru*.
- **Weapons and the duel** (for V 73–76, VI 82–91, VII): *qaštu* `קֶשֶׁת`, *mulmullu* `חֵץ`, *miṭṭu* `אַלָּה`, *išpatu* `אַשְׁפָּה`, *sapāru* `רֶשֶׁת`, *imḫullu* `רוּחַ־רָעָה`, *abūbu* (weapon) `מַבּוּל`, *narkabtu* `מֶרְכָּבָה`, *ḫepû* `בִּקַּע`, *šalamtu* `גְּוִיָּה`, *kamāru* `מִכְמֹרֶת`, *ṣerretu* `חֶבֶל הָאַף`.
- **The sky**: add *ašratu* = `זְבוּל` and *meḫretu* = `לְעֻמַּת` to corpus §3 (row *šamû*), with the note that eBL's English "firmament" (IV 141) is a false echo; *mašṭû* = `מִשְׁטָח`; *šerkuppu* (open decision 1).
- ***rēṣu* = `עוֹזֵר`** (with Job 9:13 `עֹזְרֵי רָהַב` as the standing [equivalent]).
- ***barû* = `בָּחַן`**, ***ešû* = `נָבוֹךְ`**, ***manû* = `מָנָה`** (count) / `שִׁנֵּן` (recite).

### Lessons for the guidelines
- **The edition's English can import Genesis vocabulary.** eBL renders *ašratu* (IV 141) "firmament", the Vulgate's word for `רָקִיעַ`, though the Akkadian is a plain poetic word for heaven. Read the rank-3 translation after drafting also as an echo check of its own: a familiar biblical word in a modern translation may have no basis in the original.
- **A cognate can be unavailable because the corpus already gave its Hebrew word to another lemma** (*ayyābu* ~ `אוֹיֵב`, while `אוֹיֵב` = *lemnu*, fixed by a repeated line). Then choose a distinct word and state the cognate in the note; do not break a fixed repeated line for it.
- **Prefer the cognate noun even when the verb of the same root has another Hebrew rendering** (*ṣamādu* = `רָתַם`, but *naṣmadu* = `צֶמֶד`; *ḫalāpu* stative `עָטוּי`, but *naḫlaptu* = `חֲלִיפָה`): the noun often has an exact Hebrew cognate that the verb lacks.
- **Check the stem of homographic forms against their parallel**: *uptarrira* (IV 106) is D perfect or Dt preterite; the parallel N *issapḫa* in the same line decides for the passive.
- **Hidden cognates come in clusters around one image.** Around the bow (IV 35–38): *kânu* D + *matnu* = `כּוֹנְנוּ … עַל-יֶתֶר` (Ps 11:2), *išpatu* = `אַשְׁפָּה`; around the duel (IV 97–112): *karšu* = `כָּרֵשׂ` (Jer 51:34), *parāru* = `פוֹרַרְתָּ` (Ps 74:13), *šebēru* = `שִׁבַּרְתָּ` (Ps 74:13), *kamāru* = `מִכְמֹרֶת`, *batāqu* = `בִּתֵּק`. When one cognate turns up, search the same biblical passage for the others.

## Tablet V

### Scope
Tablet V, eBL lines 1–158 (eBL has no section breaks and no old line numbers for this tablet; the Hebrew headings are ours). The most damaged tablet of the poem (eBL: 44 lines intact, 107 partial, 7 lost; 61–119 rest largely on one manuscript, HuzNA1):
- **1–11** stations (*manzāzu*) for the great gods; stars and constellations as their images; the year and its boundaries; twelve months of three stars each; Nēberu's station "to mark their bonds"; Enlil's and Ea's stations; gates in both of Tiāmat's ribs, bolts left and right; the zenith in her liver.
- **12–26** Nannaru (the moon) made to appear and entrusted with the night, "jewel of the night, to mark the days"; Marduk's charge to the moon: horns at the beginning of the month, a half-crown on the seventh day, *šapattu* (full moon) at mid-month, facing the sun, waning, the day of disappearance, conjunction on the 30th; then (broken) the sun / Šamaš, "sworn" against murder and oppression.
- **27–46** broken: lines on Šamaš, the end of the month, the day of disappearance, "he built the day", the year, New Year (*zagmukku*), regularity, a bolt of the exit, the watches of night and day.
- **47–66** the world from Tiāmat's body: her spittle into clouds; wind, rain, mist, her venom heaped up – kept in Marduk's own hand; her head heaped over, the underground waters opened, Euphrates and Tigris opened from her eyes, nostrils sealed, distant mountains heaped on her teats, springs bored, her tail bound as the *durmāḫu* ("great bond"), the Apsû under his feet, her thighs as the pegs/braces of heaven, her (other) half roofed and the earth established (62 ~ IV 138); his net spread out and emptied; "he shaped heaven and earth" (65).
- **67–76** the rites devised; nose-ropes (of the captive gods) handed to Ea; the Tablet of Destinies taken from Qingu given to Anu; the battle-gear hung up; **the eleven creatures: their weapons broken, bound at his feet; images (*ṣalmu*) of them set up at the gate of the Apsû, "never to be forgotten – let it be a sign (*ittu*)"**.
- **77–106** the gods rejoice; Laḫmu and Laḫamu, Anšar's embrace ("king of well-being"), gifts from Anu, Enlil and Ea, Damkina's exultation, Usmû appointed vizier of the Apsû; the Igigi and Anunnaki prostrate themselves and kiss his feet: "This is the king!"; Marduk, girded with the dust of combat, is anointed and robed; radiance, crown, the mace (95 = IV 37), feet set on the *mušḫuššu*, the staff of well-being; (broken) enthronement.
- **107–116** Laḫmu and Laḫamu to the Igigi: "formerly the son, now your king"; the name Lugaldimmerankia; the gods give him kingship: "whatever you command, we will do".
- **117–130** Marduk's speech: above the Apsû, opposite Ešarra, below the heavens, he will make a house, "the seat of my delight"; the gods coming up from the Apsû and down from heaven will lodge there (*nubattu*); its name will be Babylon, "houses of the great gods"; a festival there.
- **131–158** the gods' reply: "who but you has authority over what your hands built?"; "make our lodging in Babylon forever; let them bring our regular offerings"; (broken) toil, joy, the light; prostration; "formerly the son, now our king"; mace and staff; "let him make the plans, all craftsmanship … we".

Files: `annotated-V.rtl.md` (158 entries), `translation-V.rtl.md` (introduction, 9 section headings, 158 lines, 30 footnotes).

### Sources consulted
- eBL `L/1/2` SB V: `reading-edition.md` (N, the three variant reconstructions 12, 22, 61, the 29 notes, parallels; the ATF of 8, 12–22, 46, 59, 61, 62, 66, 70, 71, 75, 76, 79, 82, 83, 91, 98, 109, 114, 119, 129, 130, 142, 147, 157), `glossary.md` (all 314 entries, for the stems), `manuscripts.md` (35 manuscripts), `_raw/chapter-display.json` (checked: no `isBeginningOfSection`, no `oldLineNumbers`).
- eBL English (Foster), extracted to a separate file and read only after the Hebrew of every line had been formed.
- King 1902 vol. 1 (history only: 1–26 – "the stations", "a being of the night", "to determine six days" in 16, *[ina] māti* in 15, the Nibir/Anu variant of 8; his introduction on the Fifth Tablet and Gen 1:14–19).
- The annotated files and project sections of Tablets I–IV, the corpus and project renderings, the flood corpus renderings.
- The repository's vocalized Bible via `tools/check_bible_quotes.py --show`.
- Not used: Lambert 2013, Talon 2005, Kämmerer & Metzler 2012 (paid); Langdon 1923 (not needed beyond King for the history of the readings).

### Decisions
1. **Repeated lines**: V 95 = IV 37 copied character for character (`נשא את האַלָּה, האחיז (בה) את ימינו;`; the last sign of *ušāḫi[z]* is restored, the word is certain, so no brackets). V 62 ~ IV 138, V 73 ~ IV 115, V 74 ~ IV 111: the shared words are copied (`את מחציתה`, `קֵרָה`; `ואחד־עשר`, `מבנים`, `אשר`; `עצר`, `נשקיהם`) and only what the Akkadian changes is changed (62 no *iškunam-ma*, *erṣeta uktinna*; 73 *nabnīssa ša tiāmtu ibnû*; 74 *ḫepû* `בִּקַּע` instead of *šebēru* `שִׁבֵּר`). Internal: 126 = 128 and 134 = 136 identical; 109 / 153 differ only in *marduk* / *bēlu* (and brackets); 110 / 154 in the suffix; 125 / 127 in "from the Apsû / come up" vs "from heaven / come down"; 133 / 135 in *mimma* / *qaqqari*; 130 / 138 share only *nubattu* = `מלון`.
2. **The moon and the sun (12–29).** *nannāru* = `נַנָּרוּ` (a name, never `מָאוֹר`/`יָרֵחַ`); *šamšu* (lemma "sun", 19, 21, 22) = `שֶׁמֶשׁ`, *Šamaš* (lemma the god, 25, 29) = `שַׁמַשׁ`; **both vocalized at every occurrence**, because unvocalized they are the same word. *šūpû* = `הוֹפִיעַ`; ***qiāpu* "to entrust" = `מָסַר`** (12, 84), because *paqādu* (84) is the cognate of `פקד` and keeps `הִפְקִיד`/`פָּקַד` (fixed passage I 152). *šukuttu* = `עֲדִי` (13 *šuknat mūši* `עֲדִי הלילה`); *agû* = `כֶּתֶר`; *qarnu* = `קֶרֶן`; *nebû* = `נָגַהּ`; *sebūtu* = `(הַיּוֹם) הַשְּׁבִיעִי`; *mašlu* = `חָצוּי` (kept apart from *mišlu* `מַחֲצִית`); *šapattu* = `כֵּסֶה`; ***maḫāru* Št = `הִשְׁתַּוָּה`** (18, 22, 40; IV 142 *uštamḫir* `השווה`); *šanānu* G = `הִתְחָרָה`; *bubbulu* = `(יוֹם) הַהֵיעָלְמוּת`; *šalāšû* = `(יוֹם) הַשְּׁלוֹשִׁים`; *arḫišam* = `יֶרַח בְּיַרְחוֹ` (after `חֹדֶשׁ בְּחָדְשׁוֹ`, with *warḫu* = `יֶרַח`).
3. **The calendar (1–8)**: *manzāzu* = `מְקוֹם עֲמִידָה`; *bašāmu* = `תִּכֵּן`; *uddû* = `סִמֵּן` throughout (3, 6, 13 ×2, 16, 52, 124); *miṣru* = `גְּבוּל` with *maṣāru* D `תָּחַם` (the cognate `מֵצַר`/Aram. `מִצְרָא` noted, not used: unvocalized `מצרים` reads "Egypt"); *rašādu* Š = `יִסֵּד` (6, 98, 123); ***riksu* = `מוֹסֵרָה`** (6, 66; Ps 2:3), because `קֶשֶׁר`/`קָשַׁר` belongs to *kaṣāru* (II 2; V 49); *arnu* = `עָוֹן`; *egû* = `שַׁאֲנַן` (as III 137).
4. **Tiāmat's body (47–66).** *rupuštu* = `רִיר`; *erpetu* = `עָנָן` and *imbaru* = `עֲרָפֶל` both occur (49, 51) and are kept apart (the proposed cognacy *erpetu* ~ `עֲרָפֶל` is noted); *kaṣāru* = `קָשַׁר`; *sabāʾu* Š = `הֵנִיד` (49, 63; not `הֵנִיעַ`, since `נע` = *dâlu*); ***zanānu* Š = `הִגְשִׁים`** (with *zunnu* = `גֶּשֶׁם`), **not `הִמְטִיר`**, which would create an echo of Gen 2:5 that the Akkadian (not cognate) does not have; *kaṣāṣu* = `*זַרְזִיף*` (uncertain); *qatāru* Š = `הֶעֱשִׁין` (the cognate `קִיטוֹר` noted; `הִקְטִיר` is cultic); *šapāku* = `שָׁפַךְ` [cognate] (53, 57) because `צָבַר` is *kamāru* (51); *nagbu* = `מַעֲמַקִּים`; *šebû* = `שָׂבַע` [cognate] (54, 89); *peḫû* = `סִתֵּם`; *ezēbu* = `עָזַב` [cognate]; ***ṣertu* = `דַּד`**, not `שַׁד`, so as not to create a Hebrew `שד`/`שדי` pun the Akkadian lacks; *nambaʾu* = `מַבּוּעַ` [cognate?] (not `מַעְיָן`, Gen 7:11); *palāšu* D = `נִקֵּב`; *kuppu* = `בּוֹר`; ***rakāsu* "to bind" = `רָכַס`** [cognate] (59) – IV 85 had `חָגַר` for the Gt stative "harnessed", which does not fit binding a tail, and `קָשַׁר` is *kaṣāru*; *durmāḫu* transliterated `דּוּרְמָחוּ`; *retû* = `תָּקַע` (61 `*יתדות* השמים`); *šipru* = `פֹּעַל` (63, 141), **not `מְלָאכָה`**, although the semantic development (mission → work) is the same: the [equivalent] is in the apparatus; *patāqu* = `עִצֵּב` (65, vocalized).
5. **The images and the sign (73–76)**: *nabnītu* + suffix = `מִבְנֶיהָ` (so that `מִבְנֶיהָ … בָּנְתָה` keeps the Akkadian *nabnīssa … ibnû*); *ḫepû* = `בִּקַּע` (corpus), suitable for sticks/maces; *esēru* = `עָצַר` (IV 111); *ṣalmu* = `צֶלֶם`; *ṣabātu* Š = `הִצִּיב`; *aḫrâtaš* = `מֵעַתָּה`; *mašû* N = `נִשְׁכַּח`; *ittu* = `אוֹת`.
6. **Gifts, homage, regalia (67–106)**: *pelludû* = `חֻקּוֹת` (kept apart from *parṣu* `פֻּלְחָן`); *ṣabātu* Št "to make take hold" = `הִתְפִּיס` (68; root of G `תָּפַס`); ***tāmartu* = `שַׁי`** (70, 83) – the exact semantic equivalent is `תְּשׁוּרָה` (both from verbs of seeing; 1 Sam 9:7), but `תְּשׁוּרָה` is *igisû* (IV 134), so it is noted only; ***tabālu* = `הוֹלִיךְ`** (70) – I 68 had `נָטַל`, but project §4 gives `נָטַל` to *ekēmu* (IV 121) and both verbs stand in V 69–70; *qiāšu* = `הֶעֱנִיק`, *qīštu* = `מַתָּנָה`; *alālu* II = `תָּלָה` (as IV 38, 51); *apāru* = `עִטֵּר`; *redû* G = `נָהַג` (Št `הִנְהִיג`, II 151); *edēru* = `חִבֵּק` (as I 53); *alālu* III Š with object = `הִלֵּל`; *nawāru* Š = `הֵאִיר`; *tuqsiqqû* = `*מַחֲלָצָה*`; Usmû = `אוּסְמוּ`; *šukkallūtu* = `וְזִירוּת`; *paqādu* = `פָּקַד`; *kanāšu* = `כָּרַע`; *lalû* = `נֹעַם` (89, 122); *ebēḫu* D = `אָזַר` (90; Ps 93:1 `הִתְאַזָּר`); *tarbuʾu* = `אָבָק`; *labāku* Š = `הִשְׁרָה`; *edēqu* Dt = `הִתְעַטֵּף` (as I 68) with *tēdīqu* = `מַעֲטֶה`; *rašubbatu* = `בְּעָתָה`; *ušparu* = `מַטֶּה` (*miṭṭu* stays `אַלָּה`, *ḫaṭṭu* `שֵׁבֶט`); *azamillu* = `שַׂק`; *emāšu* transliterated `אֶמָשׁוּ`; *aštû* = `כֵּס`; *simakku* = `נָוֶה`.
7. **Kingship and Babylon (107–158)**: the one-line speech formula (108) and the two-line formula (117–118) per corpus §2; *narāmu* = `אָהוּב`; *qâlu* = `הֶאֱזִין`; Lugaldimmerankia = `לוּגַל־דִּימֶר־אַנְקִיָּה`; *zikru* = `זֵכֶר` (vocalized `זִכְרוֹ`); *kaʾinimmaku* = `*נֹסַח*`; *zanānu* II = `כִּלְכֵּל`; *ḫašmānu* = `*הַכָּחֹל*` (not `תְּכֵלֶת`, the cognate of another Akkadian word, *takiltu*); *meḫretu* = `לְעֻמַּת`; *ašratu* = `זְבוּל`; *epēšu* = `עָשָׂה` even for "making" a house (122), `בָּנָה` stays for *banû*; *nubattu* = `מָלוֹן`; *purussû* = `גְּזֵרָה`; *nabû* = `קָרָא` (129, vocalized `אֶקְרָא`); *šuma zakāru* = `הִזְכִּיר שֵׁם` (137); *nadû* of a building = `עָרַךְ` (138, as IV 1; not `הִנִּיחַ`, root `נוח`); *isinnu* = `חַג`; *sattukku* = `תָּמִיד`; *mānaḫtu* = `יְגִיעָה`; *kullumu* = `הֶרְאָה`; *nepelkû* Š = `הִרְחִיב` (148; `פָּעַר` in IV 100 was the mouth); *dâku* = `רֶצַח` (25; `הָרַג` = *nêru*); *ḫabālu* = `עֹשֶׁק`; *ummânūtu* = `אֻמָּנוּת`.
8. ***qaqqaru* as a noun (121 *qaqqarša*, 135 *eli qaqqari*) = `קַרְקַע`.** Project §4 gives *qaqqaru* = `אַרְצָה` (adverbial, II 119), which does not fit a noun with a suffix; `אֶרֶץ` is *erṣetu* (62, 65) and `אֲדָמָה` is avoided (Gen 2). `קַרְקַע` is also the rendering of *ammatu* (I 2, the only occurrence). Recorded as an open decision.
9. **Brackets** follow eBL's N by the coordinator's rule: a word is bracketed when its root, or most of it, is broken in all manuscripts (e.g. 18 `[ירח בירחו]`, 19 `[יביט]`, 22 `[בשלושים]`, 57 `[הרים]`, 62 `[מחציתה]`, 68 `[חבלי האף]`, 69 `[לוח]`, 74 `[נשקיהם]`, 75 `[בשער]`, `[הציב]`, 76 `[מעתה]`, 79 `[חיבק]`, 84 `[מסר]`, 92 `[השרה]`, 93 `[מעטה]`, 94 `[זוהר]`, 96 `[החזיק]`, 98 `[על נחש־הזעם]`, 99 `[תלה]`, 109 `[מרדוך]`, 131, 137, 138, 146, 152, 153, 156, 157); a restored ending, a restored first or last sign of a certain word, or a restored suffix is not bracketed (4, 5, 9, 17, 20, 21, 55, 58, 69 *[qi]ngu*, 77, 78, 85, 95, 127, 129, 155). eBL's `...` outside brackets is `…`, broken stretches `[…]`; one scribal omission, 77 *ḫa<dîš>*, is `⟨שמחה⟩`.
10. **eBL's English departs from its normalization** in 16 ("six days": an old reading of *zakāri*), 23 ("I d[efined?]": restored verb), 38, 45 (restored verbs), 63 ("completed his task": another verb), 68 (*[têr]ēti* "instructions" against N *[ṣerr]ēti* "nose-ropes"), 89 ("[sang his praises]"), 121 ("firmament" for *ašratu*, as in IV 141), 126/128 ("here"/"there" for the same *ašruššu*), 130 ("we shall hold" for 3 pl. *ippušū*), 142 ("[he shall rest from]": restored), 147 (*eninnu* "now", against eBL's lemma *Eninnu*, the temple; the Hebrew follows Foster, with `(?)`), 157 ("plans" for *eṣrēti*, against eBL's lemma *erṣetu* "earth"; the Hebrew follows Foster, `*מזימות*`). Each case is in the apparatus.

### Open decisions for the user
1. **121 / 135 *qaqqaru* = `קַרְקַע`** (decision 8): this shares `קרקע` with *ammatu* (I 2). Alternatives: `אֶרֶץ` (clashes with *erṣetu* in the neighbouring lines), `תַּשְׁתִּית` (modern).
2. **12 / 84 *qiāpu* = `מָסַר`** instead of the prompt's `הִפְקִיד` (reserved for *paqādu*, 84 and I 152).
3. **70 / 83 *tāmartu* = `שַׁי`** rather than the exact `תְּשׁוּרָה` (taken by *igisû*, IV 134). Alternative: move *igisû* to `מִנְחָה`/`שַׁי` and give *tāmartu* `תְּשׁוּרָה` (would change IV 134).
4. **59 *rakāsu* = `רָכַס`** (cognate) against IV 85 `חָגַר` (context "harnessed"): two Hebrew words for one lemma. Alternative: `רָכַס` everywhere (would change IV 85), or `חָגַר` here (does not fit).
5. **63 / 141 *šipru* = `פֹּעַל`** rather than `מְלָאכָה`: the semantic equivalence with `מְלַאכְתּוֹ` (Gen 2:2–3) is real (both "mission" → "work"), but the words are not cognate, so the Hebrew avoids pre-empting it. Alternative: `מְלָאכָה` with the [equivalent] tag in the note.
6. **50 *zanānu* Š = `הִגְשִׁים`**, not `הִמְטִיר` (Gen 2:5): same reasoning.
7. **57 *ṣertu* = `דַּד`**, not `שַׁד`/`שָׁדַיִם`: avoids a Hebrew-only pun on `שַׁדַּי`; the link with Gen 49:25 (`שָׁדַיִם`) is made in the apparatus.
8. **119 *ḫašmānu* = `*הַכָּחֹל*`**: alternatives a transliteration (`חַשְׁמָנוּ`) or `תְּכֵלֶת` (rejected: cognate of *takiltu*, a different word).
9. **147 *eninnu*** "now" (Foster) vs. the temple *Eninnu* (eBL lemma); **157 *eṣrēti*** "plans" (Foster) vs. *erṣetu* "earth" (eBL lemma). The Hebrew follows Foster in both, marked uncertain.
10. **25 *šamaš***: the god (eBL lemma) – `שַׁמַשׁ`; Foster translates "the sun". Lines 19–22 have the common noun. The Hebrew follows the lemmas, which makes the shift from the sun to the god visible.
11. **17 *sebūtu* = `ב(יום) השביעי`** with "(יום)" in parentheses (one Akkadian word); alternative `בַּשְּׁבִיעִי` alone.
12. **2 *tamšīlšunu*** "their likeness" taken as the gods' likeness (the stars as images of the great gods of line 1); alternative: "their (the stars') likenesses", i.e. the constellations as star-pictures (Foster: "the star images").

- 2026-10-16: read the guidelines, the corpus and project renderings, the flood corpus renderings, the Tablet I–IV sections of this file, the Tablet IV annotated and clean files (format, IV 19–20, 35, 37, 103, 111, 115, 138), the eBL edition of V (N, variants, lemmas, notes, the ATF of the doubtful lines) and the whole V glossary. eBL's English (T) was extracted to a separate scratch file and not read until the Hebrew of every line had been formed.
- 2026-10-16: drafted `annotated-V.rtl.md` in five sections (header + 1–26, 27–46, 47–66, 67–106, 107–158), then compared with eBL's English and King 1902 and wrote the comparisons into the apparatus.
- 2026-10-16, **pass (a) fidelity**: every content word checked against N. Changed: 25 a trailing `…` removed (nothing follows *ḫabāla* in N); 70 `כראשית` → `ראשית השי –` and `העניק אותו` → `העניק (אותו)` (no object suffix certain); 92 `בברוש` → `(ב)ברוש` (the preposition is not in the Akkadian). Brackets checked word by word against N (decision 9); `( )` additions checked (12, 14, 17, 21, 36, 37, 49, 52, 58, 64, 70, 71, 75, 92, 101); `*…*` for uncertain translations (20, 50, 59, 61, 66, 82, 114, 119, 157); `?` in 147.
- 2026-10-16, **pass (b) consistency**: checked by lemma across the tablet: *uddû*, *manzāzu*, *izuzzu* Š, *banû*, *bašāmu*, *eṣēru*/*uṣurtu*, *šakānu*, *kânu* D, *maḫāru* Št, *šamšu*/*Šamaš*, *qerēbu* Št, *šigaru*, *elâti*, *sabāʾu* Š, *šapāku*, *petû*, *wabālu*, *tāmartu*, *qiāpu*, *apsû*, *nubattu*, *šukênu*, *zakāru*, *qabû*, *melammu*, *rašubbatu*, *miṭṭu*, *ušparu*, *tešmû*, *šulmu*, *lalû*, *šebû*, *puḫru*, *abbūšu*, *bēlu*, *šarru*, *ašruššu*, *purussû*, *epēšu*, *šipru*, *ūmu*, *šattu*, *warḫu*. Names vocalized at their first occurrence in V only (`[מַרְדּוּךְ]` 48 is restored; Anu at 70; Laḫmu and Laḫamu at 78). Earlier tablets' Hebrew words were grepped for collisions with the new terms: changed 6 / 66 *riksu* `קשר` → `מוֹסֵרָה` (collision with *kaṣāru* `קשר`, 49); 25 *dâku* `הרג` → `רצח` (collision with *nêru*); 70 *tabālu* checked against I 68 (decision 6). Internal repeated pairs compared by `verify.py`.

| 62 | *[mišil]ša uṣṣallila erṣeta uktinna* | את [מחציתה] קרה, את הארץ כונן | `וַיֹּאמֶר אֱלֹהִים יִקָּווּ הַמַּיִם מִתַּחַת הַשָּׁמַיִם אֶל מָקוֹם אֶחָד וְתֵרָאֶה הַיַּבָּשָׁה` (בראשית א:ט); `כִּי-הוּא עַל-יַמִּים יְסָדָהּ וְעַל-נְהָרוֹת יְכוֹנְנֶהָ` (תהלים כד:ב); `יֹצֵר הָאָרֶץ וְעֹשָׂהּ הוּא כוֹנְנָהּ` (ישעיהו מה:יח) | [divergence] (Gen 1:9) / **[equivalent]** (Ps 24:2); *kânu* D = כונן [cognate] | The earth is the other half of the sea-corpse, "established" with the verb of Ps 24:2 ("established it upon the seas"). Restored *[mišil]ša*. |
| 63, 141 | *šipra; šiprīni* | המלאכה; מלאכתנו | `וַיְכַל אֱלֹהִים בַּיּוֹם הַשְּׁבִיעִי מְלַאכְתּוֹ אֲשֶׁר עָשָׂה` (בראשית ב:ב); `מִכָּל מְלַאכְתּוֹ אֲשֶׁר בָּרָא אֱלֹהִים לַעֲשׂוֹת` (בראשית ב:ג) | [equivalent] | *šipru* and `מְלָאכָה` share the semantic path "mission → work"; not cognate; the Hebrew uses `פֹּעַל`. In 141 "our work" is the gods' work that someone else must do – the lead-in to man in VI. |

At Bethel the beings are messengers, seen in a dream by a man. |

### Lines shared with other tablets
- V 95 = IV 37 (exact).
- V 62 ~ IV 138; V 73 ~ IV 115; V 74 ~ IV 111 (shared words copied).
- V 1–2 echo IV 19 (*lumāšu*); V 98 echoes I 141 (*mušḫuššu*); V 69 refers back to IV 121–122 and I 157 (the Tablet of Destinies); V 116 has the frame of I 126; V 152 has the frame of IV 20; V 112 and 152 the name Lugaldimmerankia (VI 139 ff.).
- Internal: 109 ~ 153, 110 ~ 154, 125 ~ 127, 126 = 128, 130 ~ 138, 133 ~ 135, 134 = 136.
- **Hebrew of lines that later tablets may repeat or echo**: 112 `לוּגַל־דִּימֶר־אַנְקִיָּה – זִכְרוֹ, בו בִּטְחוּ!`; 117–118 `מרדוך פתח את פיו ואמר, / אל האלים, אבותיו, דבר דיבר:`; 126 `שם יהי מלונכם, לקבל את אספתכם.`; 129 `אֶקְרָא את שמו בָּבֶל – בתי האלים הגדולים;`; 133 / 135 `על כל אשר / על הקרקע אשר בָּנוּ ידיך,`; 151 `השתחוו לו האלים, אומרים לו:`.

### Proposals for the shared renderings
For the corpus `renderings.md` (§3) or the project §4 (rows added there):
- ***qiāpu* = `מָסַר`** (V 12, 84), so that `הִפְקִיד`/`פָּקַד` stay for *paqādu* [cognate פקד]. The corpus §1 note on Nannaru could cite V 12 with `מָסַר`.
- ***maḫāru* Št = `הִשְׁתַּוָּה`** (V 18, 22, 40; IV 142 `הִשְׁוָה`) and ***šanānu* G = `הִתְחָרָה`** (V 22; IV 4 `אֵין מִתְחָרֶה`).
- **Moon vocabulary** for VII and the companions: *šukuttu* `עֲדִי`, *nebû* `נָגַהּ`, *mašlu* `חָצוּי`, *bubbulu* `(יוֹם) הַהֵיעָלְמוּת`, *šalāšû* `(יוֹם) הַשְּׁלוֹשִׁים`, *arḫišam* `יֶרַח בְּיַרְחוֹ`; and the rule: **`שֶׁמֶשׁ` / `שַׁמַשׁ` vocalized at every occurrence** when both occur in one text.
- ***riksu* = `מוֹסֵרָה`** and ***rakāsu* "to bind" = `רָכַס`** [cognate] (V 6, 59, 66); the coordinator should decide whether IV 85 (`חָגַר`, *rakāsu* Gt "harnessed") stays.
- ***tabālu* = `הוֹלִיךְ`** (V 70) vs. *ekēmu* `נָטַל` (IV 121, §4): I 68 has `נָטַל` for *tabālu*; a decision for the corpus is needed (they recur in VI–VII).
- ***tāmartu* = `שַׁי`**, with `תְּשׁוּרָה` (= *igisû*) noted as the exact equivalent.
- ***šipru* = `פֹּעַל`** (not `מְלָאכָה`); ***mānaḫtu* = `יְגִיעָה`** (with *dullu* `עָמָל`) – both matter for VI (the gods' toil laid on man) and Gen 2:2–3, 5:29.
- ***zanānu* Š = `הִגְשִׁים`** (not `הִמְטִיר`); ***zanānu* II = `כִּלְכֵּל`**.
- ***šapāku* = `שָׁפַךְ`** (also of earth and mountains), since `צָבַר` is *kamāru* in this project; a note to the flood corpus row (`שָׁפַךְ` / `יָצַק` / `צָבַר`).
- ***nambaʾu* = `מַבּוּעַ`**, ***ṣertu* = `דַּד`**, ***ḫallu* = `יָרֵךְ`**, ***zibbatu* = `זָנָב`** (body words of Tiāmat).
- ***sattukku* = `תָּמִיד`**, ***isinnu* = `חַג`**, ***pelludû* = `חֻקּוֹת`** (cult words for VI–VII).
- ***qaqqaru* (noun) = `קַרְקַע`** – see open decision 1; the coordinator may prefer to move *ammatu* (I 2) to another word.
- ***lalû* = `נֹעַם`**; ***ebēḫu* = `אָזַר`**; ***edēqu* = `הִתְעַטֵּף`**, ***tēdīqu* = `מַעֲטֶה`** (as I 68); ***ušparu* = `מַטֶּה`**; ***aštû* = `כֵּס`**; ***simakku* = `נָוֶה`**; ***emāšu*** transliterated.
- Names: Usmû = `אוּסְמוּ`; Lugaldimmerankia vocalized `לוּגַל־דִּימֶר־אַנְקִיָּה` (corpus §1 form, used).

### Lessons for the guidelines
- **A name can be the hidden echo.** *Nannaru* means "luminary": the closest lexical contact between Ee V and Gen 1:16 is a divine name that the Hebrew text has as a common noun (`הַמָּאוֹר`). Transliterating the name keeps the comparison honest, but the apparatus must say what the name means, or the echo is hidden.
- **Two words that are identical unvocalized** (`שֶׁמֶשׁ` the sun / `שַׁמַשׁ` the god) must be vocalized at every occurrence when both occur in one text, not only at the first occurrence.
- **Semantic twins without cognacy** (*šipru* / `מְלָאכָה`, *tāmartu* / `תְּשׁוּרָה`): both developed the same meaning from the same image. Record them as [equivalent] in the apparatus and in the parallels index; whether the translation uses the twin depends on whether it would pre-empt a key comparison.
- **Check new terms against the Hebrew already in the earlier tablets, not only against the renderings tables**: `קֶשֶׁר` for *riksu* collided with *kaṣāru* (II 2), `הָרַג` for *dâku* with *nêru* (I 69), and *tabālu* had already received `נָטַל` in I 68 (later given to *ekēmu* in §4). A grep of the `>` lines of all earlier tablets for every new Hebrew word is cheap.
- **When the edition's lemma and its own translation disagree on the word itself** (V 147 *Eninnu* / "now", V 157 *erṣetu* / "plans"), translate the reading that the context and the manuscripts support, mark it uncertain, and record both.
- **In a damaged tablet the modern translation's restorations can manufacture parallels**: Foster's "[he shall rest from]" (V 142) would make a Gen 2:2–3 parallel out of a lacuna. The parallels index must say so in the row itself.

## Tablet VI

### Scope
Tablet VI, eBL lines 1–166. eBL marks section breaks only in the list of names (before 123, 133, 135, 137, 139, 143, 147, 151, 155, 157, i.e. before each name); the Hebrew headings for 1–122 are ours. No old line numbers. The tablet is well preserved: only 1–4 and 16 have words broken in all manuscripts (38 manuscripts).
- **1–16** Marduk's plan: "I shall knot blood and bring bone into being, I shall make *lullû* stand – let 'man' be his name; … let the toil of the gods be laid on them, that they may rest"; he will change the ways of the gods and divide them in two. Ea's counsel: let one god, their brother, be given up, destroyed, and let people be shaped; let the guilty one be given up.
- **17–38** The assembly: "Who made the strife, made Tiāmat rebel?" – "Qingu." Qingu is bound before Ea, the guilt is laid on him, his blood is cut; from his blood Ea builds mankind and lays the gods' toil on it, releasing the gods; "that work is not fit to be understood"; Nudimmud built by Marduk's ingenuity.
- **39–54** Marduk divides the Anunnakkū above and below: 300 in heaven as a guard, as many for the earth, 600 in all; he allots their shares. The grateful Anunnakkū offer to build a dais, "Your chamber – let it be our lodging", where they will rest.
- **55–66** Marduk orders Babylon built; its bricks are moulded for a year; in the second year Esagil's head is raised, the ziggurat of the Apsû built, a seat founded for Anu, Enlil, Ea and Marduk.
- **67–81** The dedication: the gods' own shrines, the 300 Igigi and 600 of the Apsû assemble; Marduk seats the gods at a banquet ("This is Babylon, the seat of your dwelling"); a ritual in Esagil; the orders and designs established; the stations of heaven and earth distributed; the fifty great gods and the seven gods of destinies.
- **82–94** The bow: Marduk lays it before the gods; Anu kisses it ("let her be my daughter"), names it Long-wood, Reacher and Bow-star, sets it in heaven among the gods; a throne is set up for Marduk.
- **95–122** The gods' oath (a self-curse; water and oil; touching their throats), kingship given; Anšar names him Asarluḫi; the gods' decree on his rule (shepherdship of the black-headed, offerings, temples, "the likeness of what he made in heaven let him make on earth", people divided as to gods – "for us he is our god"); "let us name his fifty names".
- **123–166** The first nine names, each explained by learned etymology (table below); Anšar, Laḫmu and Laḫamu named three each and charge the gods to pronounce his names; the gods take counsel in Ubšukkinnaku. The list continues in VII.

Files: `annotated-VI.rtl.md` (166 entries), `translation-VI.rtl.md` (introduction, 17 section headings, 166 lines, 30 footnotes).

### Sources consulted
- eBL `L/1/2` SB VI: `reading-edition.md` (N, the 11 variant reconstructions, 17 notes, parallels; ATF where a reading mattered: 1–7, 16, 33, 66, 82), `glossary.md` (stems of every lemma used), `manuscripts.md` (38 manuscripts), `bibliography.md`, `_raw/chapter-display.json` (checked: no `oldLineNumbers`).
- eBL English (Foster), extracted to a separate scratch file and read only after the Hebrew of all 166 lines had been drafted (`draft.txt` in the session scratchpad).
- King 1902 vol. 1 (history only: VI 1–7 – "my blood will I take", after Berossus; the first comparison of *eṣemtu* with `עֶצֶם`).
- The annotated files and project sections of Tablets I–V; the corpus and project renderings; the flood corpus renderings and Atra-ḫasīs I 174–246 (the creation of man).
- The repository's vocalized Bible via `tools/check_bible_quotes.py --show`.
- Not used: Langdon 1923 (not needed beyond King); Lambert 2013, Talon 2005, Kämmerer & Metzler 2012 (paid).

### Decisions
1. **The creation of man (5–8, 32–38, 129–130), vocabulary:** *dāmu* = `דָּם` (singular, as in Atra-ḫasīs I 206 `בבשרו ובדמו`, although the Akkadian is plural); ***kaṣāru* = `קָשַׁר`** (5 `דם אֶקְשֹׁר`; also 24/30 `קשר מלחמה`), not `צָבַר` (= *kamāru*, §4); *eṣemtu* = `עֶצֶם` [cognate]; *šubšû* = `הֵבִיא לִכְלַל הֲוָיָה` (the verb of Tiāmat's monsters, I 146); *lullû* = `קַדְמוֹן`, *amēlu* = `אָדָם`, *amēlūtu* = `הָאֱנוֹשׁוּת`, *nišū* = `הָאֲנָשִׁים`; *banû* = `בָּנָה`; *patāqu* N = `יְעֻצְּבוּ` (14).
2. ***emēdu* "to lay on, impose" = `הֵשִׁית (עַל)`** (8 passive `יוּשַׁת`, 32, 34, 36, 130): the biblical verb of imposing a penalty or sin (`אַל נָא תָשֵׁת עָלֵינוּ חַטָּאת`, Num 12:11; Ex 21:22). Not `הֵטִיל` (= *nadû*) and not `הֶעֱמִיס` (*našû* Št in the fixed passage). The commonly proposed cognacy *emēdu* ~ עמד is noted as [cognate?].
3. ***arnu* = `עָוֹן`** (16, 26, 32; project §4, V 7), not `אָשָׁם`. 26 *arnuššu lušaššâ* = `את עוונו אַשִּׂיא (אותו)` – the Hebrew idiom of Lev 22:16 (`וְהִשִּׂיאוּ אוֹתָם עֲוֹן אַשְׁמָה`), same verb (*našû* Š = `נשא` Hiphil) and same object.
4. ***wuššuru* = `שִׁלַּח`** (34), as in the flood corpus and IV, not `שִׁחְרֵר`; the release of the gods from corvée is compared with `שַׁלַּח אֶת עַמִּי` (Ex 5:1).
5. ***šipru* = `פֹּעַל`** throughout (37, 57, 67), never `מְלָאכָה`; the [equivalent] with `מְלַאכְתּוֹ` (Gen 2:2–3) is made explicit in the apparatus of 37 and 67 and in footnote 9 (both words develop "mission, sending" → "work": *šapāru* "to send" / לאך "to send").
6. **The division of the gods (10, 39–46, 79, 119, 145):** *zâzu* = `חִלֵּק` everywhere (including 119, the division of mankind "as to gods"); ***isqu* "lot, share" = `חֵלֶק`** (46; `גּוֹרָל` is *šīmtu*): the resulting `חילק את חלקם` is a Hebrew-only figura (stated in the note); *maṣṣartu* = `מִשְׁמֶרֶת` (42); ***ḫamšat šušši* = `חֲמֵשׁ שִׁשִּׁיּוֹת`** (42, 69: the sexagesimal form kept, "= 300" in the note), *nēr* = `שֵׁשׁ מֵאוֹת` (44, 69: a unit word, rendered as the number). **No `צָבָא`**: there is no *ṣābu* in the tablet.
7. **Babylon (49–66):** *šubarrû* = `פְּטוֹר` (49; `דְּרוֹר` stays for *andurāru*); *dumuqqû* = `תּוֹדָה`; *parakku* = `דּוּכָן`; *kummu* = `חֶדֶר`; *nubattu* = `מָלוֹן`; *pašāḫu* Š intransitive = `נַרְגִּיעַ` (52, 54; Isa 34:14 `הִרְגִּיעָה`); *nēmedu* = `מִשְׁעָן`; *nadû* (a building) = `עָרַךְ` (53, 93, 144); *zīmu* = `זִיו` (56; `פָּנִים` = *pānu*); *epēšu* = `עָשָׂה` (57, not "build"); *libittu* = `לְבֵנָה`, *labānu* = `לָבַן` (60 `לָבְנוּ`) [cognate ↔ Gen 11:3]; *zaqāru* = `זָקַף` (58); *allu* = `מַעְדֵּר`; *tarāku* = `הָלַם`; *rēša ullû* = `הֵרִים אֶת רֹאשׁוֹ` (62; the line explains the name Esagil, Sum. *é-sag-íl* "house whose head is raised"); *ziqqurratu* = `זִקּוּרַת`; *elû* adj. = `רָם` (63 `הָרָמָה`, keeping the link with 62 `הרימו`); *tarbītu* = `גְּדֻלָּה`; *narmû* = `מָדוֹר` (72; `מְעוֹן` = *gipāru*, `נָוֶה` = *simakku*); *nagû* / *nigûtu* = `רִנֵּן` / `רִנָּה`; *zarbābu* = `גְּבִיעִים`; *taqribtu* = `הַקְרָבָה` (root *qrb* kept without deciding between "offering" and "intercession"); *paramāḫu* = `דּוּכָן־הַנַּעֲלֶה`.
8. **The bow (82–94):** *qaštu* = `קֶשֶׁת` [cognate]; *kakkab qašti* = `כּוֹכַב הַקֶּשֶׁת`; *wapû* Š = `הוֹפִיעַ` (90; Št passive `יוּפְעוּ` 118; Š stative `מוּפַעַת` 122); ***gisgallu* = `תַּחֲנָה`** (91; `מְקוֹם עֲמִידָה` stays for *manzāzu*); *atḫû* = `אַחִים` (91 `אֶחָיהָ`: the suffix is feminine – the bow's brothers, not Anu's as in Foster); *nâdu* = `שִׁבַּח` (85, 136; `הִלֵּל` = *alālu* Š). The three names: `"עֵץ־אָרֹךְ"`, `"מַשִּׂיג"` (*kāšid*), `"כוכב הקשת"`.
9. **The oath (97–98):** *arāru* = `מְאֵרָה` (noun; [cognate] ארר); *zakāru* D = `הִזְכִּיר` (97, not `אָמַר`); *tamû* = `נִשְׁבַּע`; *lapātu* = `לָפַת` [cognate?]; *napištu* = `נֶפֶשׁ` (here "throat", with the note).
10. **The gods' decree (99–122):** *enūtu* = `גְּבִירוּת` (106; `אֲדָנוּת` = *bēlūtu*, 100); ***rēʾûtu* = `רוֹעוּת`** (107; not `רְעִיָּה`, because `רעיית` already renders *ḫīrtu*, I 78); ***binītu* = `בְּנוּיִים`** (107, 152; root `בנה` kept, distinct from *nabnītu* `מִבְנִים` and *binûtu* `מִבְנֶה`); *ṣalmāt qaqqadi* = `שְׁחוֹרֵי הָרֹאשׁ` (flood corpus), with the false-friend note *ṣalmu* "black" ≠ *ṣalmu* "image"; *aḫrâtaš ūmī* = `לְאַחֲרִית הַיָּמִים` (108; *aḫrâtaš* alone = `מֵעַתָּה`, 136); *nindabû* = `מִנְחָה`; *zāninūtu* = `כַּלְכָּלָה`; *eṣēnu* Š = `הֵרִיחַ` (causative sense, 111); *qutrinnu* = `קְטֹרֶת`; *diʾu* = `בִּימָה`; *riāšu* Š = `הֵשִׂישׂ`; *tamšīlu* = `תַּבְנִית` (112); *baʾūlātu* = `נְתִינִים`; *puqqu* D = `הִקְשִׁיב` (19, 103, 115); *mašālu* G stative = `מָשׁוּל`.
11. **113 *uddû* = `הוֹדִיעַ`** – a stated deviation from corpus §3 (`סִמֵּן`): here the verb takes a person and an infinitive ("let him teach the black-headed to fear him"), which `סִמֵּן` cannot carry; `הוֹדִיעַ` is from ידע, the cognate of *idû*. *palāḫu* = `פָּחַד` (§4, IV 108), not `יָרֵא` as the prompt suggested (`יָרֵא` = *adāru*, II 90).
12. **Speech and naming verbs:** *nabû* = `קָרָא` only (51, 88, 101, 120, 121, 123, 139, 147, 151, 154, 155, 157, 159, 165); its derivative *nību* = `קְרִיאָה` (21); *zakāru* D / *šuma zakāru* = `הִזְכִּיר (שֵׁם)` (97, 108, 143, 160, 166; N `יוּזְכְּרוּ` 114); *zakāru* Gt = `דִּבֵּר`; *zikru* "name" = `זֵכֶר` (51, 102), "utterance" = `מַאֲמָר` (1, 140, 161). *i* + precative 1 pl. = `הבה` + future (51–54, 102, 121, 154, 164): 121 is `הבה נקרא את חמישים שמותיו` (the prompt's `נִקְרָא נָא` would be ambiguous with the N stem unvocalized).
13. **Other new terms:** *alkakātu* = `הֲלִיכוֹת` (9, 43; Ps 68:25; `דֶּרֶךְ` stays for *alaktu*); *nakālu* D = `חִכֵּם`; *kabātu* D = `כִּבֵּד`; *šanû* III Št = `שָׁנָה` (43, "did again"; 9 *šanû* IV D = `שִׁנָּה`, eBL's lemma, with Foster's "double" in the note); *nabalkutu* Š = `הִמְרִיד`; *kittu* = `נְכוֹנָה` (22; Ps 5:10); *malku* = `נָגִיד` (28; `מֶלֶךְ` = *šarru*); *eršu* = `פִּקֵּחַ` (35; `חָכָם` = *apkallu*, `נָבוֹן` = *ḫasīsu*); *gimratu* = `כְּלָלוּת`; *merītu* = `מִרְעֶה`, *mašqītu* = `מַשְׁקֶה` [cognate], *urû* = `אֻרְוָה` [cognate], *ṭaḫādu* D = `דִּשֵּׁן` (124); *šāpû* = `סוֹרֵר` (125, a sixth enemy word); *šapšāqu* = `מְצוּקָה`; *kayyāna* = `בִּקְבִיעוּת`; *šikittu* = `מַתְכֹּנֶת` (129; Ex 30:32); *napšu* = `נֶפֶשׁ`; *napšuru* = `לִמְחֹל`; *enēnu* = `הֶעֱנִישׁ`; *nannû* = `מִצְוָה`; *palāsu* N = `הִבִּיט`; *tukultu* = `מִבְטָח`; *šabāsu* = `רָגַז`; *tayyāru* = `מִתְרַצֶּה`; *muštālu* = `שׁוֹקֵל`; *rapāšu* = `רָחַב`; *lâṭu* = `*בּוֹלֵם*`; *taklimtu* = `מַרְאֶה`; *adāru* Š stat. = `מְבֻעָתִים`; *ašāru* = `סָקַר`; *pušqu* = `מֵצַר`; *raʾābu* = `חָרַד` (146; `רָעַד` is *tarāru*, IV 90, 108); *nâšu* = `זָע` (`נָע` = *dâlu*); *gešṭû* = `חָלוּץ`; *lamassu* = `אֵלַת־מָגֵן`; *nêšu* Š = `מְחַיֶּה` (Piel; *balāṭu* D = `הֶחֱיָה`); *kašāru* = `תִּקֵּן`; *abtu* = `הָרוּס`; *egru* = `עִקֵּשׁ`; *ellu* / *mullilu* = `טָהוֹר` / `מְטַהֵר`; *zāninu* = `מְכַלְכֵּל`; *nadānu* Št = `נָתַן (זה לזה)`; *mēsû* = `טְקָסִים`; *miliktu* = `עֵצָה`.
14. **Names:** transliterated from eBL's normalization, hyphenated by Sumerian elements where these are clear, vocalized at the first occurrence in the tablet (table below). ***Asarluḫi* = `אֲסַרְלוּחִי`** (eBL *asarluhi*; Foster and most literature *Asalluḫi*): open decision 1. ***Marukku*** (eBL) = `מַרוּכּוּ`, not *Marukka* (Foster). The text's own explanations are translated as they stand (127 *māru šamši* = `הבן – שֶׁמֶשׁ האלים` explains *amar-utu*; 141 explains Lugal-dimmer-ankia; 62 explains Esagil).
15. **Repeated lines:** 24 = 30 identical, including the final dash and the vocalized `תִּיאָמַת`/`הִמְרִיד` in both (as in the vocalized pairs of III). 15 / 95 differ only in tense (precative / preterite, the one difference `verify.py` reports). Internal "cf." pairs share their Hebrew word for word where the Akkadian agrees: 1 ~ 55 (`מרדוך, בשומעו …`), 23 ~ 25 ~ 29 (`אשר בנה את התגרה`), 13 ~ 25 (`יִנָּתֵן לִי`), 8 ~ 34 ~ 130 (`עמל האלים`, `הֵשִׁית`, `והם`), 33 ~ 129 (`בנה`), 19 ~ 103 ~ 115 (`למעשה פיו … יקשיבו`), 52 ~ 54 (`הבה נַרְגִּיעַ בקרבו!`), 105 ~ 163 (`המשיב גמולנו`), 123 ~ 147 (`אשר … קרא לו אביו, אנו`), 144 ~ 150 (`את מושבנו בַּמֵּצַר`). 127 ~ I 102 uses I 102's words (`הבן`, `שֶׁמֶשׁ`, `האלים`); 2 = IV 136's `בונה מְחֻכָּמוֹת`; 37 = I 94's `להבין … נאה`; 7 follows Atra-ḫasīs I 183 (`… קדמון – אדם`).
16. **Brackets:** only 1 `[מַר]דּוּךְ` (partial: one syllable of two restored), 3 `[מעשה]` (most of *epšu* restored), 4 `[את אשר]`; 2 *[ub]bal* and 16 *[š]a* (prefix / one sign) unbracketed, as the פירוק says.
17. **Readings where the Hebrew departs from eBL's English:** 33 and 130 (singular subject, with the normalization, against Foster's plural / and with Foster against the long *ū* in 130 – both noted); 66 (*inaṭṭala* singular: "he looks toward its horns", against Foster's "its pinnacles were facing", marked `?`); 81 (*uktinnū* active D, left ambiguous); 89 (*lū kāšid* a name "Reacher", not a wish); 91 (feminine suffix: the bow's brothers); 114 (with Foster: N "be invoked"); 152 (*kīma binûtīšu* "as his own creatures", against Foster's "in accordance with its meaning"); 154 (eBL's reading *i nibbûšu*, not von Soden's "let us praise"); 137 (*muštāl* "deliberating" against eBL's lemma *šâlu* II "to rejoice").

### Open decisions for the user
1. **Asarluḫi**: `אֲסַרְלוּחִי` (eBL's normalization *asarluhi*) or `אֲסַלּוּחִי` (the common reading *Asalluḫi*, Foster). The same question for VII (the name recurs).
2. **113 *uddû* = `הוֹדִיעַ`** (a deviation from corpus §3 `סִמֵּן`, decision 11). Alternative: keep `סִמֵּן` and accept an unidiomatic line, or adopt `הוֹדִיעַ` as the rendering of *uddû* with a personal object corpus-wide.
3. ***emēdu* = `הֵשִׁית`** (decision 2), which brings Num 12:11 into view. Alternative: `הֵטִיל` (Atra-ḫasīs I 237 `הטלתי על האדם`) – but `הֵטִיל` is *nadû* in Enūma eliš.
4. **46 *isqu* = `חֵלֶק`**: the Hebrew `חילק את חלקם` creates a figura that the Akkadian (*zâzu* … *isqu*) lacks. Alternative: `מָנָה` (`חילק את מנתם`).
5. **5 *dāmī***: singular `דם` (as the flood corpus) against the Akkadian plural; `דָּמִים` would be literal but carries the Hebrew sense "bloodshed, blood-guilt".
6. **42, 69 *ḫamšat šušši***: `חמש שישיות` (the sexagesimal form) or simply `שלוש מאות`.
7. **66** crux (decision 17): Marduk looking at Esagil's horns (our reading of the form) or the horns facing the base of Ešarra (Foster).
8. **138 *lāʾiṭ karassu***: `*בולם* בטנו` ("restraining his feelings") or Foster's "all-encompassing" (`מקיף`). The line recurs in VII 155.
9. **107 *rēʾûtu* = `רוֹעוּת`** (modern-sounding but transparent) vs. `רְעִיָּה` (homograph of *ḫīrtu*'s `רעייתו`) or a verbal rendering `ירעה את שחורי הראש` (loses *epēšu*).
10. **35 *eršu* = `פִּקֵּחַ`** (to keep `חָכָם` for *apkallu*); Atra-ḫasīs I 188 has `החכמה` for *erištu*. The corpus may want one word for *eršu* in both.
11. **The names' hyphenation**: `מֶר־שָׁא־כּוּשׁוּ` is hyphenated by a Sumerian analysis (*mer-šà-kúš-ù*) that is commonly given but not certain; `מַרוּכּוּ` and `מַרוּתוּכּוּ` are not hyphenated (no clear analysis). Tablet VII should follow one policy.

| 39–40 | *uzaʾʾiz … eliš u šapliš* | חילק … ממעל וממטה | `וַיַּבְדֵּל בֵּין הַמַּיִם אֲשֶׁר מִתַּחַת לָרָקִיעַ וּבֵין הַמַּיִם אֲשֶׁר מֵעַל לָרָקִיעַ` (בראשית א:ז) | [divergence] | An upper/lower division: of gods here, of waters in Gen 1. *zâzu* ≠ בדל. |
| 42, 44, 69 | *ḫamšat šušši ina šamê ukīn maṣṣarta; nēr* | חמש שישיות בשמים כונן למשמרת; שש מאות | `וַיְכֻלּוּ הַשָּׁמַיִם וְהָאָרֶץ וְכָל צְבָאָם` (בראשית ב:א); `כֹּל צְבָא הַשָּׁמַיִם` (דברים ד:יט) | [divergence] | Heaven and earth populated by counted gods (300 + 300); a "guard" (*maṣṣartu*), **not a *ṣābu* "host"** – `צָבָא` is not used. |
| 119, 145 | *lū zīzā-ma ṣalmāt qaqqadi ilī; uzaʾʾizu manzāza* | יהיו מחולקים שחורי הראש – (לפי) אלים; חילק מקום עמידה | `אֲשֶׁר חָלַק יְהוָה אֱלֹהֶיךָ אֹתָם לְכֹל הָעַמִּים תַּחַת כָּל הַשָּׁמָיִם` (דברים ד:יט) | [equivalent] / [divergence] | Mankind divided by gods (each people its god) as an act of the supreme god; Deut uses the same idea to forbid Israel the host of heaven. |
| 49 | *šubarrâni taškunū* | את פטורנו שמת | `וּקְרָאתֶם דְּרוֹר בָּאָרֶץ לְכָל יֹשְׁבֶיהָ` (ויקרא כה:י) | [equivalent] | A royal decree of exemption from labour. |
| 51–54 | *i nīpuš parakka … kummuk lū nubattani i nušapšiḫ qerbuš* | הבה נעשה דוכן … "חדרך – יהי מלוננו!" הבה נרגיע בקרבו | `זֹאת-מְנוּחָתִי עֲדֵי-עַד פֹּה-אֵשֵׁב כִּי אִוִּתִיהָ` (תהלים קלב:יד); `וַיָּנַח בַּיּוֹם הַשְּׁבִיעִי` (שמות כ:י) | [equivalent] | The rest after man's creation gets a place: the shrine in Babylon (*pašāḫu*, not *nâḫu*). |
| 51, 57 | *i nīpuš parakka ša nabû zikiršu; epšā-ma bābili* | הבה נעשה דוכן אשר נקרא זכרו; עשו את בבל | `הָבָה נִבְנֶה לָּנוּ עִיר וּמִגְדָּל וְרֹאשׁוֹ בַשָּׁמַיִם וְנַעֲשֶׂה לָּנוּ שֵׁם` (בראשית יא:ד) | [equivalent] / **[divergence]** | "Let us make", a name, Babylon: built by the gods at the god's command and for him, vs. by men for themselves (and scattered). |
| 58, 60 | *libnassu lippatiq; šattu ištât libittašu iltabnū* | תעוצב לבנתה; שנה אחת – את לבנתה לבנו | `הָבָה נִלְבְּנָה לְבֵנִים וְנִשְׂרְפָה לִשְׂרֵפָה` (בראשית יא:ג) | **[cognate]** | Same verb + same noun (*labānu libitta* = נלבנה לבנים) in the building of Babylon. Here moulded mud-brick; in Gen burnt brick. |
| 62 | *ša esagil … ullû rēšīšu* | את אסגיל … הרימו את ראשו | `וּמִגְדָּל וְרֹאשׁוֹ בַשָּׁמַיִם` (בראשית יא:ד) | [cognate] (*rēšu* = ראש) / [equivalent] | The line etymologizes Esagil, "house whose head is raised"; praise here, hubris in Gen. |
| 63, 66 | *ziqqurrat apsî elīta; šuršiš ešarra … qarnīšu* | זקורת האפסו הרמה; בשורש אשרה – מביט(?) אל קרניו | `וּמִגְדָּל וְרֹאשׁוֹ בַשָּׁמַיִם` (בראשית יא:ד) | [divergence] | The temple-tower reaching the base of heaven (66 a crux); Gen calls it `מִגְדָּל`. |
| 72 | *annâ bābili šubat narmîkun* | זוהי בבל, מושב מדורכם | `אֵין זֶה כִּי אִם בֵּית אֱלֹהִים וְזֶה שַׁעַר הַשָּׁמָיִם` (בראשית כח:יז); `עַל כֵּן קָרָא שְׁמָהּ בָּבֶל כִּי שָׁם בָּלַל יְהוָה שְׂפַת כָּל הָאָרֶץ` (בראשית יא:ט) | [equivalent] / [divergence] | Babylon as the gods' house (folk etymology *bāb ilī* "gate of the gods" ↔ `שַׁעַר הַשָּׁמָיִם`); Gen 11:9 counter-etymology from בלל. |
| 82, 87, 90–91 | *imḫur-ma bēlu qašta kakkašu maḫaršun iddi; šī lū mārtī; kakkab qašti ina šamê ušāpi; ukīn-ma gisgallaša* | קיבל האדון את הקשת – את נשקו לפניהם הטיל; היא – תהי בתי; "כוכב הקשת" – בשמים הופיע; כונן את תחנתה | `אֶת קַשְׁתִּי נָתַתִּי בֶּעָנָן וְהָיְתָה לְאוֹת בְּרִית בֵּינִי וּבֵין הָאָרֶץ` (בראשית ט:יג) | [cognate] (*qaštu* = קשת) / **[divergence]** | After a war in/with the waters the god's bow is laid down and set in heaven: here a weapon adopted as Anu's "daughter" and made a star among the gods; in Gen a bow in the cloud as a covenant sign. |
| 88–90 | *ibbī-ma ša qašti kīam šumīša* | קרא את שמותיה של הקשת כך | `וַיִּקְרָא אֱלֹהִים לָאוֹר יוֹם` (בראשית א:ה); `וְכֹל אֲשֶׁר יִקְרָא לוֹ הָאָדָם נֶפֶשׁ חַיָּה הוּא שְׁמוֹ` (בראשית ב:יט) | [equivalent] | Naming as fixing a thing's status (*nabû* = קרא by rendering, not cognate). |

| 98 | *itmû ulappitū napšāti* | נשבעו, לפתו נפשות | `כִּי בָאוּ מַיִם עַד-נָפֶשׁ` (תהלים סט:ב) | [cognate] (*napištu* = נפש "throat") | Touching the throat in an oath. |
| 107 | *rēʾût ṣalmāt qaqqadi binâtuššu* | רועות שחורי הראש, בנוייו | `וּרְדוּ בִּדְגַת הַיָּם וּבְעוֹף הַשָּׁמַיִם וּבְכָל חַיָּה` (בראשית א:כח); `יְהוָה רֹעִי לֹא אֶחְסָר` (תהלים כג:א) | [divergence] / [equivalent]; false friend *ṣalmu* "black" ≠ צלם | The god shepherds the men he built; in Gen man rules the animals. `בְּצַלְמֵנוּ` has no counterpart here (*ṣalmāt* = "black"). |

| 111 | *lišēṣin qutrinnī* | יריח קטורת | `וַיָּרַח יְהוָה אֶת רֵיחַ הַנִּיחֹחַ` (בראשית ח:כא) | [equivalent]; *qutrinnu* = קטרת [cognate] | The gods smell the offering. |
| 112 | *tamšīl ina šamê īteppušu ina erṣeti līteppuš* | את התבנית אשר עשה בשמים – בארץ יעשה | `וּרְאֵה וַעֲשֵׂה בְּתַבְנִיתָם אֲשֶׁר אַתָּה מָרְאֶה בָּהָר` (שמות כה:מ); `אֵת תַּבְנִית הַמִּשְׁכָּן` (שמות כה:ט); `נַעֲשֶׂה אָדָם בְּצַלְמֵנוּ כִּדְמוּתֵנוּ` (בראשית א:כו) | **[equivalent]** (Ex) / [divergence] (Gen) | Earthly works made after a heavenly *tamšīlu* (`תַּבְנִית`, the project's fixed rendering); in Gen 1:26 the "likeness" is man himself. Borger's emendation *līteppuš* (followed by eBL). |

| 125 | *ina kakkīšu abūbi ikmû šāpûti* | בנשקו, המבול, אסר את הסוררים | `וַאֲנִי הִנְנִי מֵבִיא אֶת הַמַּבּוּל מַיִם עַל הָאָרֶץ` (בראשית ו:יז) | [equivalent] / [divergence] | *abūbu* is Marduk's weapon against the gods' enemies, not a flood on mankind. |
| 127 | *lū māru šamši ša ilī nebû šū-ma* | יהי הבן – שֶׁמֶשׁ האלים – נוגה הוא | `אֶת הַמָּאוֹר הַגָּדֹל לְמֶמְשֶׁלֶת הַיּוֹם` (בראשית א:טז) | [divergence] | The text's etymology of Marduk (*amar-utu* "son/calf of the sun"); Gen does not name the sun. ~ I 102. |

| 129 | *nišī ša ibnû šikitta napša* | האנשים אשר בנה – מתכונת (של) נפש | `וַיְהִי הָאָדָם לְנֶפֶשׁ חַיָּה` (בראשית ב:ז); `וְאֵת כָּל נֶפֶשׁ הַחַיָּה הָרֹמֶשֶׂת` (בראשית א:כא) | **[cognate]** (*napšu* ~ נפש) / [equivalent] | Man as a built "form" that has *napšu* "breath, life"; in Gen 2:7 the נפש comes from God's breath. |
| 131 | *banâ abāta napšura enēna* | לבנות, להרוס, למחול, להעניש | `לִנְתוֹשׁ וְלִנְתוֹץ וּלְהַאֲבִיד וְלַהֲרוֹס לִבְנוֹת וְלִנְטוֹעַ` (ירמיהו א:י); `אֲנִי אָמִית וַאֲחַיֶּה מָחַצְתִּי וַאֲנִי אֶרְפָּא` (דברים לב:לט) | [cognate] (*banû* = בנה) / [equivalent] (*abātu* / הרס) | Pairs of opposites as the scope of divine authority; Jer 1:10 has the same pair as infinitives. |
| 133 | *lū ilu bānûšunu* | יהי האל, בונם | `הֲלוֹא הוּא אָבִיךָ קָּנֶךָ הוּא עָשְׂךָ וַיְכֹנְנֶךָ` (דברים לב:ו) | [equivalent] | The creator (*bānû*, "builder") as lord of his creatures. |
| 137 | *eziz (u) muštāl sabuš (u) tayyār* | זועם ושוקל, רוגז ומתרצה | `שׁוּב מֵחֲרוֹן אַפֶּךָ וְהִנָּחֵם עַל הָרָעָה` (שמות לב:יב); `וַיֹּאמֶר יְהוָה אֶל לִבּוֹ לֹא אֹסִף לְקַלֵּל עוֹד אֶת הָאֲדָמָה` (בראשית ח:כא) | [equivalent] | The angry god who relents. |

| 151, 153 | *ilu mušneššu; uballiṭu ilī mītūti* | האל המחיה; החיה את האלים המתים | `אֲנִי אָמִית וַאֲחַיֶּה` (דברים לב:לט) | [equivalent] / [absent] | Dead gods revived: no biblical counterpart. |

### Lines shared with other tablets
- VI 127 ~ I 102 (`הבן – שֶׁמֶשׁ, שמש הא[לים]!` → `יהי הבן – שֶׁמֶשׁ האלים – נוגה הוא!`; the shared words copied).
- VI 138 = VII 155 (first occurrence here): `רחב לבו, *בולם* בטנו.`
- VI 2b ~ IV 136b (`בונה מְחֻכָּמוֹת`); VI 37 ~ I 94a (`להבין … נאה`); VI 48 ~ IV 20, V 152 (`… הם דיברו:`); VI 105, 163 ~ IV 13 (`המשיב גמולנו`); VI 83 ~ IV 41 (*sapāra epēšu*); VI 7 ~ Atra-ḫasīs I 183 (flood corpus).
- Internal: 15 ~ 95 (tense), 24 = 30, 23 ~ 25 ~ 29, 13 ~ 25, 1 ~ 55, 8 ~ 34 ~ 130, 33 ~ 129, 19 ~ 103 ~ 115, 52 ~ 54, 105 ~ 163, 123 ~ 147, 144 ~ 150.
- **Hebrew of lines that Tablet VII may repeat or echo**: 121 `הבה נקרא את חמישים שמותיו:`; 138 `רחב לבו, *בולם* בטנו.`; 139 `"לוגל־דימר־אנקיה" – שמו, אשר קראה אספתנו;`; 166 `בטקסים – כולם הזכירו את שמו:`; the name frame `"X" – שמו, אשר קרא לו אביו, אנו;` (147).

**The names of Marduk in Tablet VI** (Tablet VII continues the table):

| 1 | 123 | *marduk* | מַרְדּוּךְ | *amar-utu(k)* "calf (= son) of the sun" | שם מרעה ומשקה …; יהי הבן – שֶׁמֶשׁ האלים (127) |
| 2 | 133 | *marukku* | מַרוּכּוּ | uncertain (Foster *Marukka*) | יהי האל, בונם הוא, המיטיב … המרגיע |
| 3 | 135 | *marutukku* | מַרוּתוּכּוּ | uncertain; sound-play *-tuk-* / *tukultu* | יהי מבטח הארץ, העיר ואנשיה |
| 4 | 137 | *meršakušu* | מֶר־שָׁא־כּוּשׁוּ | *mer* "fury" + *šà* "heart" + *kúš-ù* "to soothe" | זועם ושוקל, רוגז ומתרצה |
| 5 | 139 | *lugaldimerankiʾa* | לוּגַל־דִּימֶר־אַנְקִיָּה | *lugal* "king" + *dimmer* "gods" + *an-ki-a* "of heaven and earth" | יהי אדון אלי השמים והארץ, כולם (141) |
| 6 | 143 | *nari-lugaldimerankiʾa* | נָרִי־לוּגַל־דִּימֶר־אַנְקִיָּה | *na-ri* "instruction"(?) + 5 | הסוקר את האלים כולם; ערך את מושבנו |

### Proposals for the shared renderings
For the corpus `renderings.md` (§3) or the project §4 (rows added there):
- ***emēdu* = `הֵשִׁית (עַל)`** (the gods' toil, guilt); flood corpus Atra-ḫasīs I 237 has `הטלתי` – the corpus should choose one verb for the two creation-of-man texts (open decision 3).
- ***arnu* = `עָוֹן`** confirmed for corpus §3, with the idiom *arna šuššû* = `הִשִּׂיא עָוֹן` (Lev 22:16).
- ***šipru* = `פֹּעַל`** to corpus §3 as a Gen 2:2–3 key term ([equivalent] `מְלָאכָה`, the "sending" etymology), so that the companions (Atra-ḫasīs uses `מלאכה` for *šipru*, I 180, 197, 233) can be aligned – **a real inconsistency between the two corpora** on the key word for `מְלַאכְתּוֹ`.
- ***isqu* = `חֵלֶק`**; ***gisgallu* = `תַּחֲנָה`**; ***narmû* = `מָדוֹר`**; ***taqribtu* = `הַקְרָבָה`**; ***nindabû* = `מִנְחָה`** (check against the flood corpus *surqinnu* = `מִנְחָה`: a collision if both occur in one text); ***mēsû* = `טְקָסִים`**.
- ***alkakātu* = `הֲלִיכוֹת`**; ***binītu* = `בְּנוּיִים`**; ***rēʾûtu* = `רוֹעוּת`**; ***enūtu* = `גְּבִירוּת`**; ***šikittu* = `מַתְכֹּנֶת`**; ***napšu* = `נֶפֶשׁ`**.
- **Emotion / fear verbs**: *šabāsu* = `רָגַז` (with *ezēzu* `זעם`, *agāgu* `קצף`, *kamālu* `כעס`, *labābu* `זעף`); *raʾābu* = `חָרַד` (*tarāru* `רעד`); *nâšu* = `זָע` (*dâlu* `נע`); *adāru* G `יָרֵא`, Š `הִבְעִית`; *palāḫu* `פָּחַד`.
- **Enemy words**: add *šāpû* = `סוֹרֵר`, *egru* = `עִקֵּשׁ`.
- **Names**: Asarluḫi (open decision 1); `מַרוּכּוּ`, `מַרוּתוּכּוּ`, `מֶר־שָׁא־כּוּשׁוּ`, `נָרִי־לוּגַל־דִּימֶר־אַנְקִיָּה`, `אֲסַרְלוּחִי־נַמְתִּילָה`, `אֲסַרְלוּחִי־נַמְרוּ` for corpus §1; Esagil's meaning ("house whose head is raised") to the corpus §1 note, since VI 62 is the text's own explanation.
- ***uddû* with a personal object = `הוֹדִיעַ`** (VI 113; open decision 2).

### Lessons for the guidelines
- **A creation verb shared across a text is the strongest hidden echo**: *šubšû* ("bring into being") is used for Tiāmat's monsters (I 146) and for man (VI 5), exactly the two objects of `בָּרָא` in Gen 1 (1:21, 1:27). A per-lemma index of the whole poem (all `>` lines with their lemmas) makes such distributions visible; a line-by-line reading misses them.
- **Check a new Hebrew word against the Hebrew of the earlier tablets as a string, not only as a lemma**: `רְעִיַּת` (shepherdship) is spelled like `רעיית` (consort, I 78); `ירעדו` was already *tarāru*. An index file of all earlier `>` lines (tablet, line, normalization, Hebrew, parse) grepped for each new word catches both kinds of collision in seconds.
- **Watch the two corpora for the same key word in different Hebrew**: Atra-ḫasīs renders *šipru* `מְלָאכָה`, Enūma eliš `פֹּעַל`. A corpus-level table of the Gen 1–3 key terms with their rendering in *every* text would prevent it.
- **The text's own etymologies are data**: in VI the poem explains Esagil (62), Marduk (127) and Lugal-dimmer-ankia (141) by translating their Sumerian elements; translating the explanation literally, and saying in the note which element it renders, keeps the reader able to check the learned etymology.

## Tablet VII

### Scope
Tablet VII, eBL lines 1–164 = **166 entries** (eBL numbers 62a/62b and 121a/121b separately; the prompt's "1–166" is the entry count, the last line is 164). eBL marks **no section breaks** and gives **no old line numbers** for this tablet (checked in `_raw/chapter-display.json`); the Hebrew headings are ours, one per name, as in Tablet VI. 54 witnesses (24 library/school tablets, 30 commentaries, quotations and god lists). Only 163–164 are badly broken.
- **1–135** the names 10–50 (the list continues from VI 123–156), each with the poem's own explanation: Asarre, Asaralim, Asaralimnunna (1–8: cultivation, barley and flax, "who brings forth the green"); Tutu and its four sub-names (9–34: purification, the spell, rest of the gods, mankind built to release the defeated gods, reviving the dead); Šazu and five sub-names (35–56: knower of hearts, truth and falsehood distinguished, the enemies uprooted); Enbilulu and four sub-names (57–69: irrigation, pasture and watering, the *edû*-flood, furrows, "who rains abundance, makes the green sprout"); Sirsir, Malaḫ (70–77: the mountain heaped on Tiāmat, Tiāmat his "vehicle" and he her sailor); Gil, Gilima, Agilima, Zulum, Mummu, Zulummu (78–88: grain and ewe, the seed of the land, the earth built above the waters, "builder of heaven and earth"); Gišnumunab … Kinma (89–108: maker of all people from the defeated gods' substance, the gods' bond, counsellor of Ea); Dingiresiskur, Girru, Addu, Ašaru (109–123: house of prayer, the gods' gifts, the four quarters of mankind his creatures, the storm that covers the sky and gives food below); **Nēberu** (124–135: the crossing of heaven and earth, his star, the courses of the stars, the gods shepherded like sheep, Tiāmat bound for ever).
- **136–142** the extra names: Enlil gives him his own title "Lord of the Lands"; Ea rejoices and gives him his own name "Ea" with the bond of his rites and all his instructions.
- **143–162** the epilogue: the fifty names "by the word 'Fifty'"; they are to be kept and taught (the "first one" shows them, the wise confer, the father repeats to the son, shepherd and herdsman open their ears) so that the land flourishes; his word is firm, no god can face his anger; the text was written down "for the later ones to hear"; his name is to be invoked wherever water is drunk; "the song of Marduk, who bound Tiāmat and took kingship".
- **163–164** a broken colophon-like end (Anu, Enlil, Ea, Bēlet-ilī; Babylon and Esagil).

Files: `annotated-VII.rtl.md` (166 entries), `translation-VII.rtl.md` (introduction, 48 section headings, 166 lines, 30 footnotes).

### Sources consulted
- eBL `L/1/2` SB VII: `reading-edition.md` (N, the 7 variant reconstructions, 9 notes, parallels; the ATF of every line whose N has brackets, and of 18, 40, 88, 91, 121a, 159, 162–164), `glossary.md` (the stems of every verb used), `manuscripts.md`, `_raw/chapter-display.json` (no sections, no old numbers).
- eBL English (Foster/Fadhil), extracted to a separate scratch file and read only after the Hebrew of all 166 lines had been drafted.
- eBL texts named in the `Parallels:` field, fetched once from the eBL API to identify them (not saved in `sources/`): **L II.1 = the Babylonian Theodicy** (279–280: the gods gave mankind "twisted speech … lies and not truth"; the prompt's "Ludlul?" is wrong), **L III.11 = the Syncretistic Hymn to Marduk** (*Eriš šummi*; 59 "who brought barley and flax into being", 60 "the furrow … tillage granted to him"). "Marduk 5 14" and "LB 3272 Vs. 5" were not identified further.
- King 1902 vol. 1 (VII 1–5, 109–115 in his numbering = eBL 1–5, 129–135) and Langdon 1923 (VII 127–139 = eBL 146–158), history of reading only.
- The annotated files and project sections of Tablets I–VI, an index of all earlier `>` lines with their normalizations (scratch file), the corpus, project and flood renderings.
- The repository's vocalized Bible via `tools/check_bible_quotes.py --show`.
- Not used: Lambert 2013, Talon 2005, Kämmerer & Metzler 2012 (paid).

### Decisions
1. **Names (table below).** Transliterated from eBL's normalization and vocalized at the first occurrence; a hyphen joins a sub-name to its main name as eBL does (*tutu ziʾukkina* = `טוטו־זיאוכנה`, as VI *asarluhi-namtila*), and an internal hyphen is used **only at a clear Sumerian word boundary** (*asar-*, *lugal-*, *en-*, *šà-zu*, *dingir-*, *ir-*, *dumu-*, and the *X-rim / X-gurim* pairs). This answers Tablet VI's open decision 11 with one policy. *Tutu* = `טוּטוּ` (the same god as in the flood corpus's `אוּבָּר־טוּטוּ`); *Addu* = `אַדּוּ` (= flood corpus `אֲדַד`, noted); *Malaḫ* = `מַלָּח` (the word *malāḫu* itself, [cognate] `מַלָּח`); *Mummu* = `מוּמּוּ` (corpus §1); *Nēberu* = `נֵבֶרוּ` (corpus §1). "Lord of the Lands" (136) and "Ea" (140) are **translated** in the text, like the bow's names in VI 89–90, because they are Akkadian titles, not Sumerian names.
2. **Counting the names.** Our count runs from VI's nine (Marduk … Asarluḫi-Namru; VI 101 Asarluḫi not counted) to **Nēberu = 50**, counting Zulummu (87) and not counting Lugalduku (100, a separate god or title). "Lord of the Lands" and "Ea" are extra names (51–52), and 143 ("by the word 'Fifty'") ties the fifty to Enlil's number. Foster numbers Zulummu and Gišnumunab both "35", so his count from 36 on is one lower (Nēberu = 49, "Lord of the World" = 50). The headings say "השם ה־N".
3. **The ordinal formulas:** *šanîš / šalšiš / rebîš* = `שנית / שלישית / רביעית`; *ina šanî / ina rebî / ina ḫamši / ina šešši* = `בשני / ברביעי / בחמישי / בשישי` (different Akkadian, different Hebrew). Naming verbs as Tablet VI: *nabû* = `קָרָא` (19, 76, 136, 137, 144; *nabû* adj. "called" = `קָרוּא`, 58; *nibûtu* = `קְרִיאָה`, 102); *zakāru* Gt/N = `הִזְכִּיר` / `הוּזְכַּר` (15, 50, 61, 160); *zikru* "name" = `זֵכֶר` (54, 137, 139), "utterance" = `מַאֲמָר` (143).
4. **The Genesis vegetation verbs are rendered by their cognates**, because the Akkadian words are the cognates and the meanings match (guidelines §6.4 rule 2): *šūṣû* (Š of *aṣû*) = `הוֹצִיא` and *urqītu* = `יֶרֶק` (2 `הַמּוֹצִיא יֶרֶק`); ***dešû* D = `הִדְשִׁיא`** (57 `מַדְשִׁיאָם`, 69 `מַדְשִׁיא יֶרֶק`; Dt 150 `תֻּדְשָׁא`); *zēru* = `זֶרַע` (79); *mēreštu* = `חֲרִישׁ` [cognate *erēšu* / `חרש`] (1, 62b); *palgu* = `פֶּלֶג` (63); ***edû* = `אֵד`** (61, the accepted etymon of Gen 2:6 `אֵד`); ***rukūbu* = `רְכוּב`** (77, = Ps 104:3); *laḫru* = `רָחֵל` (79); *tīlu* = `תֵּל` (78); *šalgu* = `שֶׁלֶג` (82); *nāqidu* = `נוֹקֵד` (148); *markasu* = `רֹכֶס` (95, from *rakāsu* `רָכַס`). Where the cognate would create an echo the Akkadian does not have, it is avoided: *zanānu* Š = `הִגְשִׁים` (69, not `הִמְטִיר`), *berātu* = `*אגמים*` (60, not `מִקְוֶה`), *ṣīru* = `נַעֲלֶה` (96, not `עֶלְיוֹן`), *wussû* Dt = `מֻפְלִים` (40, not `הִבְדִּיל`).
5. **Rest:** *pašāḫu* = `נִרְגַּע` (10 = VI 8 `והם – יֵרָגְעוּ!`), *nâḫu* = `נָח` (11 `יָנוּחוּ`; 128 *lā nâḫiš* `ואינו נח`). No `שבת`, `מנוחה` or other rest word elsewhere in the tablet.
6. ***tiāmtu* in 74 and 128** is lemmatized by eBL as the common noun "sea" (tiāmtu I), while 70, 71, 77, 90, 91, 103, 116, 132, 162 have the goddess (Tiamat I). Following corpus §1 ("when the eBL lemma is the common noun *tâmtu* 'sea', use `יָם`"), 74 and 128 have `הַיָּם`, with a note and footnote 11 (after her death Tiāmat *is* the sea). Foster has "Tiamat" in both (open decision 2).
7. **VII 155 is not identical to VI 138.** eBL and project §3 list VII 155 = VI 138, but the Akkadian differs (VI 138 *rapaš libbašu lāʾiṭ karassu*; VII 155 *rūqu libbašu rapaš karassu*). Following "differ only where the Akkadian differs", the shared words are copied (`לבו`, `בטנו`, `רחב`) and the rest follows VII: `רחוק לבו, רחב בטנו,` (*rūqu* = `רָחוֹק` as *libbu rūqu* `הלב הרחוק`, II 61, VII 118). This is the one difference `verify.py` reports.
8. **Brackets checked against the manuscripts, not only against N.** eBL's N brackets several words that one manuscript preserves: 12 *[irass]un* (SipNB1 *i-ra-at-su#-un*), 159 *u[ll]û* (SipNB1 *ul-lu#-u₂#*), 162 *tiā[mta i]kmû* (SipNB1 *ti#-amta# ik#-mu#*); by the bracket rule (broken in *all* manuscripts) they are not bracketed. Brackets remain only in 163 (`[… אנ]ו`, `[…]`) and 164 (`[…]`, `אֵסַגִּי[ל …]`). One-sign restorations (6, 7, 9, 39, 40, 41, 64, 69, 81, 88, 91) are not bracketed and the פירוק says so.
9. **eBL lemma slips overridden:** 131 *ṣēni* lemmatized as *ṣēnu* "evil" – it is *ṣēnu* "sheep" (`כצאן`); 34 *lemnūti* lemmatized as *lemuttu* "evil" – the form is the masc. pl. of *lemnu* "evil ones" (`הרעים`); 62b *ṣēri* lemmatized as *ṣēru* "back" – here "steppe" (`עֲרָבָה`); 111 *imaḫḫarūni* is singular (Neo-Assyrian subjunctive *-ūni*), as Foster has it, not plural.
10. **Readings where the Hebrew departs from Foster:** 3 *kabti* agrees with *milki* ("the weighty counsel"), not with the god; 10 *lillil* is G ("let their shrines be pure"), not D ("he shall purify"); 76 *šī lū kīam* introduces 77 ("she shall be thus: Tiāmat is his vehicle"), not "so be it" after the name; 95 *šarru* is nominative (apposition "the king, the bond of the gods"), not construct; 132 *lisīq u likri* are G intransitive with *napištaša* as subject ("her life be narrowed and shortened"); 134 Marduk subject of *liššī* only, Tiāmat of *uktāl* and *lirēq* (Foster: Marduk throughout; open decision 5); 135 Foster's "firmament" for *ašru* is a false echo of `רָקִיעַ` (as *ašratu* in IV 141; King 1902 already showed from commentary R. 366 that *ašru* here means "heaven"); 143–144 the line break kept (Foster moves "The Fifty" to 144); 161 Foster adds a verb ("Let them sound abroad"), the Akkadian has only *inannam-ma* "now".
11. **New terms (all checked as strings against the `>` lines of I–VI):** see the rows added to `renderings.md` §4. The main families: praise verbs (*dalālu* `פֵּאֵר`, *šarāḫu* D `הִדֵּר`, *šamāru* Gt `סִלְסֵל`, *zamāru* `זִמְרָה`, beside *nâdu* `שִׁבַּח`, *alālu* `הִלֵּל`/`הִתְהַלֵּל`, *nagû* `רִנֵּן`); abundance words (*ḫegallu* `שֶׁפַע`, *nuḫšu* `עֲתֶרֶת`, *ṭuḫdu* `דֶּשֶׁן`, *maʾdû* `רֹב`, *ṣimru* `הוֹן`, *kubuttû* `כָּבוֹד`, *mašrû* `עֹשֶׁר`, *išpiku* `אָסָם`); irrigation and field words (*gugallu* `מְמֻנֶּה`, *miṭirtu* `תְּעָלָה`, *palgu* `פֶּלֶג`, *īku* `סוֹלְלָה`, *absinnu* `תֶּלֶם`, *apkīsu* `*חָרִיץ*`, *šerʾu* `מַעֲנָה`, *qerbetu* `כַּר`, *rîtu* `מַרְעִית`, *nawû* `נָאוֹת`, *edû* `אֵד`); the eighth enemy word *zāwiānu* = `מְשַׂנֵּא`; *raggu* = `רָשָׁע`; *šarāku* = `הִנְחִיל` (1, 73); *dabābu* = `הֶגֶה` / `הָגָה` (39, 157); *etguru* = `מְפֻתָּל`; *wussû* = `הִפְלָה`; *mā* = `כָּךְ:`; *emqu* = `מַשְׂכִּיל`; *māliku* = `מְיַעֵץ`; *šaṭāru* = `כָּתַב`; *bêlu* = `שָׁלַט`; *šupêlu* = `הֶחֱלִיף`; *nekelmû* = `הִקְדִּיר (פָּנִים)`; *šâbu* = `רָטַט`; *kadrû* = `מַשְׂאֵת`; *erbu* = `הַכְנָסָה` (keeps the link with *erēbu* Š `הִכְנִיס`).
12. ***šanû* D in 147 = `יְתַנֶּה`** (project §4: *šanû* D "repeat" = `תִּנָּה`), which shows the cognate link with Mishnaic `שָׁנָה` / Aramaic `תְּנָא` "to teach by repetition". The apparatus states that `שִׁנֵּן` (Deut 6:7) is **not** the cognate (שנן "to sharpen"), only the semantic [equivalent].

### Open decisions for the user
1. **Asarluḫi / Asarre**: Tablet VI's open decision 1 (`אֲסַרְלוּחִי` vs `אֲסַלּוּחִי`) bears on VII 1–5 too (*Asarre*, *Asaralim*, *Asaralimnunna* = `אֲסַרֵּה`, `אֲסַר־אָלִים`, `אֲסַר־אָלִים־נוּנָּה`, following eBL's *asar-*).
2. **74, 128 `הַיָּם` vs `תיאמת`** (decision 6): we follow eBL's lemma and the corpus rule; Foster (and the prompt) read the goddess. A reader of Gen 1:2 may prefer the name. Either way the note gives *tiāmtu* ~ `תְהוֹם`.
3. **The cognate renderings of decision 4** (`הַמּוֹצִיא יֶרֶק`, `מַדְשִׁיא יֶרֶק`, `אֵד`, `רְכוּבוֹ`, `זֶרַע`) put Genesis vocabulary into the text. They are the cognates with matching meanings (guidelines §6.4 rule 2), but they are the densest Genesis echoes in the poem; the alternative would be neutral words (`מצמיח צמחייה`, `גאות`, `כלי שיט`) with the cognates in the notes only.
4. **155** (decision 7): `רחוק לבו, רחב בטנו,` differs from VI 138 because the Akkadian differs. The coordinator may want to remove the pair from project §3 (it is "cf.", not "=").
5. **134**: subjects of *liššī-ma lā uktāl lirēq ana ṣâti* (Marduk / Tiāmat, decision 10) – `יִשָּׂא (אותה), ולא תּוּחְזַק, תִּרְחַק לָעַד!` vs Foster's "He shall proceed unhindered, he shall persevere till distant days".
6. **121a *mummu erpēti***: `*שאון* העננים` after the ancient commentaries (*mummu* = *rigmu*); the alternative is to transliterate `מוּמּוּ העננים` (the eBL lemma is the name Mummu). *lištakṣibam-ma* `יַקְצִיב(?)` is a guess.
7. **The name count** (decision 2): Nēberu = 50 (ours) or 49 (Foster); "Lord of the Lands" = extra (ours) or 50 (Foster).
8. **Hyphenation of names** (decision 1): one policy for VI–VII; VI's `מֶר־שָׁא־כּוּשׁוּ` fits it (*šà* boundary), VI's unhyphenated `מַרוּכּוּ`/`מַרוּתוּכּוּ` too.
9. ***šarāku* = `הִנְחִיל`** (1, 73): this brings in the root of `בְּהַנְחֵל עֶלְיוֹן גּוֹיִם` (Deut 32:8), already a parallel of *zâzu* (VI). Alternative: `חָנַן` / `זִכָּה`. `נָתַן` (= *nadānu*) and `הֶעֱנִיק` (= *qiāšu*) are taken.
10. ***ašru* (135) = `*המקום*`** (literal, with the note "= heaven") or `*השמים*`/`*המרום*` in the text (sense).
11. ***mā* = `כָּךְ:`** (128, 139) to keep it apart from *umma* `לֵאמֹר` (III 15); alternative `לֵאמֹר` for both.

### Lines shared with other tablets
- VII 155 ~ VI 138 (eBL and project §3 give "="; the Akkadian differs: decision 7).
- Shared phrases, copied word for word: VII 10b = VI 8b (`והם – יֵרָגְעוּ!`); 12b ~ I 140b (`ישיבו את חזם לאחור`); 5b ~ II 9 (`האב, מולידו`); 26, 34 ~ VI 153 (`הקסם הטהור`, `אשר בקסמו הטהור`); 29b = VI 33b (`בנה את האנושות`); 30 ~ II 62 (`אשר (הכוח) … – יש עמו`); 32b ~ V 133 (`אשר בָּנוּ ידיך/ידיו`); 33 ~ I 62 (`את לחשו הטהור`); 37b ~ VI 134 (`המיטיב את לב`); 59 ~ VI 124 (`מרעה/מרעית ומשקה`); 107b = VI 4 (`נותן עצה`); 108 ~ VI 146 (`לשמו – … האלים`); 112, 116 ~ VI 2b, IV 136b (`בונה מחוכמות`); 113 ~ VI 107 (`שחורי הראש, בנוייו`); 117 ~ I 18 (`רחב אוזן`); 118 ~ II 61 (`הלב הרחוק`); 122 ~ VI 149 (`אשר כשמו`); 126 ~ VI 90 (`בשמים הופיע`); 144 ~ VI 121 (`חמישים שמותיו`); 162 ~ I 159 (`ולקח את`). eBL's external parallels: 2 (Marduk 5 14; cf. the Syncretistic Hymn 59), 39–40 (Theodicy 279–280), 73 (Syncretistic Hymn 60), 93 (LB 3272 Vs. 5).

### Proposals for the shared renderings
For the corpus `renderings.md` (§3) or the project §4 (rows added there):
- **Gen 1–2 vegetation and water cognates** to corpus §3 as key terms: *dešû* D = `הִדְשִׁיא` [cognate ↔ `תַּדְשֵׁא`, Gen 1:11]; *urqītu* = `יֶרֶק` [cognate, Gen 1:30]; *aṣû* Š = `הוֹצִיא` [cognate ↔ `וַתּוֹצֵא`, Gen 1:12]; *zēru* = `זֶרַע` [cognate]; *edû* = `אֵד` [cognate, Gen 2:6]; *mašqītu* = `מַשְׁקֶה` [cognate]; *palgu* = `פֶּלֶג`; *rukūbu* = `רְכוּב` [cognate, Ps 104:3]. These matter for project 2 (Enki and Ninḫursaĝ, the Song of the Hoe), where the same words recur.
- ***ṣēru* = `עֲרָבָה`** (= Sum. *edin*, flood corpus rule) also for the Akkadian word.
- ***mēreštu* / *erēšu* = `חֲרִישׁ` / `חָרַשׁ`** [cognate].
- **Names**: the 41 names of the table above for corpus §1; *Tutu* = `טוּטוּ` (link with `אוּבָּר־טוּטוּ`); *Addu* = `אַדּוּ` (= `אֲדַד`); the hyphenation policy of decision 1.
- **Praise verbs** (one Hebrew root each): *nâdu* `שִׁבַּח`, *alālu* Š/Št `הִלֵּל`/`הִתְהַלֵּל`, *nagû* `רִנֵּן`, *dalālu* `פֵּאֵר`, *šarāḫu* D `הִדֵּר`, *šamāru* Gt `סִלְסֵל`; *zamāru* `זִמְרָה` [cognate, Ex 15:2].
- **Enemy words**: add *zāwiānu* = `מְשַׂנֵּא` and *raggu* = `רָשָׁע`.
- **Corpus §1 Tiāmat row**: add that VII 74 and 128 are lemmatized as the common noun (`הַיָּם`), so the rule is applied there (open decision 2).
- **Project §3**: correct "VI 138 | VII 155" to "cf." (decision 7).
- **`verify.py`**: eBL's `Parallels:` field marks VII 155 / VI 138 as exact, though the Akkadian differs; the script could compare the two normalizations and print "Akkadian differs" next to such pairs, so that a justified difference is visible as such.

### Lessons for the guidelines
- **Check the edition's brackets against the manuscripts.** eBL's normalization brackets words that a single manuscript preserves (VII 12, 159, 162: all preserved in SipNB1); a bracket rule "broken in all manuscripts" must be applied to the ATF, not to the normalization.
- **A list of epithets is dense with cognates.** The fifty names are almost all participles and nouns of everyday life (sowing, watering, herding, grain, seed, furrow, canal); these are exactly the words of Gen 1:11–12, 29–30 and 2:5–15, and many have Hebrew cognates (`הוציא`, `ירק`, `דשא`, `זרע`, `אד`, `פלג`, `משקה`, `רחל`, `נקד`). Read such lists word by word against the Genesis lexicon; the narrative tablets have fewer.
- **"Equal" parallels in the edition's list may not be equal.** eBL's parallel-lines list gives VII 155 = VI 138, but the lines share only three words. Compare the normalizations before copying a repeated line.
- **eBL's `Parallels:` sigla can be resolved with the API** (`/api/texts/L/2/1` → *Theodicy*; `/api/texts/L/3/11` → *Syncretistic Hymn to Marduk*); a sibling text sometimes holds the closest parallel (the Hymn's "barley and flax" for VII 2).

### Whole-poem observations
The poem opens with a world that does not yet exist because it is not yet named: "when on high the heavens had not been named (*lā nabû*), below the earth had not been called by name (*šuma lā zakrat*)" (I 1–2), and the gods themselves come into being as they are "named" (I 10). It closes with the opposite movement: a single god who receives fifty names (VI 121 – VII 144), plus the titles of the two older gods (VII 136, 140), so that the naming that once brought the world into being is now concentrated in one ruler, and every name is a function in the world (cultivation, watering, judgement, the stars). Naming in Enūma eliš is therefore both creation (I) and enthronement (VI–VII), and the same two verbs (*nabû* = `קָרָא`, *šuma zakāru* = `הִזְכִּיר שֵׁם`) carry both. Genesis 1 keeps the first function and drops the second: God names day, night, heaven, earth and seas (`וַיִּקְרָא אֱלֹהִים לָאוֹר יוֹם`, Gen 1:5, 8, 10), which fixes what they are, but nobody names God, and the plants, stars and animals are left unnamed (or named only by kind). Gen 2:19–20 transfers the act of naming to the creature (`וַיִּקְרָא הָאָדָם שֵׁמוֹת לְכָל הַבְּהֵמָה`), as Ee VI–VII transfers it to the gods; but where the gods name their creator to make him king, man names the creatures below him. The content of the names in VII also mirrors Gen 1's third and fourth days: the god who "brings forth the green" and "makes the green sprout" (VII 2, 69: `הוציא`, `הדשיא`, `ירק`), gives "the seed of the land" (79), waters the land with the *edû*-flood (61; Gen 2:6 `אֵד`), builds the earth above the waters (83) and fixes the courses of the stars (130) – everything that Gen 1 assigns to God's word, without the names, and that Gen 1:11–12 assigns to the earth itself (`תַּדְשֵׁא הָאָרֶץ`, `וַתּוֹצֵא הָאָרֶץ`).

## Coordinator's review (2026-10-16, after Tablet VII)
- **Line counts:** I 162, II 162, III 138, IV 146, V 158, VI 166, VII 166 = **1,098** annotated entries and 1,098 translation lines, in eBL order (`verify.py`: 0 problems).
- **Repeated lines:** the fixed passage (Tiāmat's army, 34 lines × 4 copies) is identical in all copies (0 mismatches; the copies in III carry their own brackets). eBL lists 14 further pairs whose Hebrew differs; each was checked, and each follows a difference in the Akkadian (vocative, name, mood/tense, pronoun, *bēlu*/*marduk*; VII 155 / VI 138 share only three words). 108 pairs or fixed-passage copies differ only in brackets (allowed).
- **Echo scan:** no `>` line in any tablet contains `רקיע`, `תהום`, `תנין`, `ברא`, `הבדיל`, `שבת`, `צבא`, `מאור` or `תבה` (script over all 1,098 lines).
- **Bible quotations:** `check_bible_quotes.py` reports 0 problems on all annotated and translation files, `translation.rtl.md`, `project.md`, both `renderings.md` files and `../parallels-index.md`.
- **Generated files:** `translation.rtl.md` (`build_translation.py`, header and tablet introductions in `build_translation--head.rtl.md` and `build_translation--intros.rtl.md`; 0 problems) and the table of `../parallels-index.md` (`build_parallels_index.py`; 296 rows + 16 Atra-ḫasīs I cross-references).
- **Coordinator decisions taken during the run** are listed under "Decisions (coordinator…)" above (I 156 wording; the bracket policy; *wabālu* and the III 56 exception; the Tablet I terms binding for II–VII).
- **Not done / for a later session:** no Hebrew rank-3 check (no modern Hebrew translation available); Lambert 2013 not consulted (paid); the old-numbering concordance (King, Langdon, ANET) is given only where a parallel depends on it.
